# Policy reaction (central banks, governments)

1185 claims from 191 sources. Each line links to the claim inside its source note.


## [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets]]
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C01-1|MT001-C01-1]] — Outside forces (fiscal and monetary policy, money printing, technology, war) shape financial markets, so portfolios should be built from these forces rather than from market-internal relationships.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C03-1|MT001-C03-1]] — The Fed's biggest error before the crisis was allowing mortgages with almost no money down, which biased risk-taking because borrowers risked little of their own money.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C03-6|MT001-C03-6]] — Even though the same data points no longer work, one can still read whether the Fed and Treasury are stimulating or tightening, which defines the monetary regime.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C04-1|MT001-C04-1]] — When the Fed is hiking rapidly (2022: 1% to 4%) that is the time to worry about equity exposure.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C04-2|MT001-C04-2]] — Coming into this year he expected no monetary policy change because inflation left the Fed little room to cut short rates despite presidential pressure.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C05-2|MT001-C05-2]] — Government bond markets are the most manipulated or paradigm-stuck (Greek yields below Bunds before the euro crisis, Japanese yields staying low as debt rose), and such disconnects can persist; he admits he does not understand them.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C08-3|MT001-C08-3]] — Nuclear demand is policy-driven and policy turned bipartisan and global in favour of nuclear, supporting multi-year demand from power needs.

## [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327]]
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C01-2|MT002-C01-2]] — Fixed income and currency moves in 2024 were driven by shifting monetary-policy expectations, which gave trend followers choppy rather than persistent trends in those sectors.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C05-2b|MT002-C05-2b]] — Trade policy figures favouring a weaker dollar conflict with a Treasury Secretary who needs a stable dollar to fund the deficit, so dollar direction depends on which voice dominates.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C09-2|MT002-C09-2]] — A 2020 regime shift in monetary policy (QE/QT) boosted idiosyncratic outlier trends and benefited classic trend followers in 2024.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C13-3|MT002-C13-3]] — The September 2024 Fed cut was followed by higher long yields, a mistake in assuming Fed cuts shift the whole curve down; macro trends and market trends differ.

- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C12-1|MT003-C12-1]] — Trend following is hurt by zero-rate environments and by narrow spreads across G7 markets, because the carry components vanish, not by multistrategy trading.

## [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play]]
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C01-1|MT004-C01-1]] — After inflation has been above target for 5+ years, a central bank can no longer look through supply shocks because rising inflation expectations feed wages and prices and become self-locking, which justifies a hike.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C01-3|MT004-C01-3]] — A healthy labor market gives a central bank room to hike pre-emptively as insurance against inflation without much risk to growth.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C03-2|MT004-C03-2]] — Walsh accepts QE only as an emergency tool when markets seize up, and opposes continued QE to strengthen growth because it adds little at high cost in balance sheet size and asset prices.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C03-4|MT004-C03-4]] — Guha expects Walsh to wait for stable bond markets and lower yields before restarting a plan to shrink the balance sheet gradually.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C03-5|MT004-C03-5]] — The 10-year Treasury yield rose from about 4.5% in July to about 5.25% at the time of recording, a very large move.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C04-5|MT004-C04-5]] — Uncertainty over how far the Fed will hike, with no stated roadmap, adds to upward pressure on bond yields.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C05-1|MT004-C05-1]] — Central-bank communication about its reaction function (what it watches and how it would respond) makes policy more effective and lets businesses plan, which is why consensus moved to more guidance over 30 years.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C06-1b|MT004-C06-1b]] — The Fed statement's easing/tightening bias (first forward guidance, late Greenspan) signaled whether the next move was more likely a hike or a cut.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C10-2|MT004-C10-2]] — The crisis toolkit built by Bernanke (market backstops, QE, forward guidance) was reused in the pandemic, so central banks can reliably stabilize seized markets with the same tools.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C10-3|MT004-C10-3]] — Keeping policy rates low for longer than expected after the financial crisis fostered a fuller labor-market recovery, because structural issues held back the economy.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C11-1|MT004-C11-1]] — An aggressive backstop of private-sector borrowing in March 2020 prevented a 2008-scale financial crisis.
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C11-3|MT004-C11-3]] — Rapid 2022 hikes plus QE shutdown achieved a soft landing that most economists thought unreachable, contrary to recession consensus.

## [[MT005 - A Brief World Economic History For Gold, Silver, and Fiat Money]]

## [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388]]
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C10-2|MT008-C10-2]] — Financial repression in the 1940s pinned long rates while allowing inflation, giving bondholders negative real returns until the Fed regained independence.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C11-1|MT008-C11-1]] — Employment benchmark revisions (about 1 million fewer jobs) give an administration reason to run the economy hotter.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C12-1|MT008-C12-1]] — Lower rates because of debt concerns would run the economy hot and fuel asset bubbles.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C12-X1|MT008-C12-X1]] — A Warsh mix of smaller Fed balance sheet with lower policy rates would likely push the Treasury toward more bill issuance to exploit low short rates, with SLR changes to make banks hold more Treasuries.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C13-1|MT008-C13-1]] — The Fed chair outcome could change the monetary regime: smaller balance sheet, fiscal dominance, or continued deficits; uncertainty is high.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C13-R1|MT008-C13-R1]] — Alan recalls Warsh calling the Fed's slowness to raise rates in 2021-22 the biggest macro forecasting mistake in 40 years and blaming the Fed for financing deficit expansion, consistent with Cochrane's view.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C13-X1|MT008-C13-X1]] — Counter to the literal hawkish reading of Warsh, he argues AI is disinflationary and allows lower rates, and his past stance has shifted with the party in power, so his policy is uncertain.

## [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399]]
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C04-3|MT011-C04-3]] — An energy price spike from the Iran war removed the disinflation-driven rate-cut narrative, since central banks cannot cut into rising energy-driven inflation.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C05-2|MT011-C05-2]] — An oil-price spike from Middle East conflict is an adverse supply shock (aggregate supply shifts left), producing stagflationary pressure that hurts both bonds and equities.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C05-4|MT011-C05-4]] — In 2008 the ECB hiked into an oil spike and was cutting by year-end, showing central banks can mis-respond to energy shocks.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C05-5|MT011-C05-5]] — Central banks normally look through energy shocks unless they spill into second-round effects.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C06-2|MT011-C06-2]] — Fed chair candidate Walsh and the administration use a 1990s new-economy analogy to justify lower rates on AI productivity.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C06-3|MT011-C06-3]] — In the 1990s Greenspan eventually hiked because productivity booms also generate a demand boom from investment, so AI capex is an inflationary impulse that offsets the supply disinflation.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C07-5|MT011-C07-5]] — Equities can do well in inflationary periods until interest rates rise enough to bite, so what matters is the policy rate response.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C10-3|MT011-C10-3]] — After the Fed began cutting, longer-end yields rose, so inflation expectations can matter more than the short end for market opportunity.

## [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407]]
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C03-X1|MT016-C03-X1]] — The 2026 inflation/oil shock briefly offered long-vol opportunities in oil and rates, but policy quickly controlled and compressed that volatility, cutting the payoff short.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C04-5|MT016-C04-5]] — The US reacts late to crises but once it decides to act it moves aggressively, and that is when regimes change; accumulated pressures are now forcing action.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C05-3|MT016-C05-3]] — The equity market is too big to fail: a two-quarter decline above 20% would drain liquidity and growth too much, so policy will not tolerate it.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C05-7|MT016-C05-7]] — Only a reserve-currency issuer can print money at scale without a currency collapse, so the US can do what weaker countries cannot.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C05-R2|MT016-C05-R2]] — The administration is publicly announcing its plan (Treasury backstop, sovereign wealth fund), so the policy direction can be read from officials' statements.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C06-3|MT016-C06-3]] — A state-funded equity fund short-circuits the populism of a K-shaped economy by giving everyone ownership instead of taxing the rich directly.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C08-1|MT016-C08-1]] — Large-scale government equity buying will start after the Nov 2026 midterms and before June 2027, aiming to get markets moving ahead of the 2028 election.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C08-2|MT016-C08-2]] — The administration deliberately lets markets fall and then reverses to rally them, so expect repeated drop-then-rally cycles.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C08-4|MT016-C08-4]] — The Iran deal holds only until the midterms; expect full re-engagement and renewed oil/commodity volatility from November to June.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C08-X2|MT016-C08-X2]] — Contrasting view (attributed to another guest): the administration will simply govern by executive order after the midterms; Karsan disagrees, arguing the dollar-trade objective keeps Iran engagement going.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C09-1|MT016-C09-1]] — After the midterms the administration will move to the centre by linking markets to populism (Trump and Sanders converging), which unifies both sides behind the plan.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C09-2|MT016-C09-2]] — The risk is infighting over who gets what; a crisis is what unites people, hence a messy November to June.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C09-3|MT016-C09-3]] — AI hyperscalers borrow to fund huge capex because of an implicit government backstop (buying their stock, bailing them out), and not playing along risks punishment.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C10-2|MT016-C10-2]] — Valuations are a bubble that only holds because of government support (Fed, Treasury); without it the outcome is bad, so the question is whether they retain control.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C11-1|MT016-C11-1]] — With the whole US system (Treasury, Fed, government stakes) behind the market, do not bet against it in the short to medium term.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C11-2|MT016-C11-2]] — Mean reversion applies in a natural market, but a state-managed market is probably not natural, so valuation-based reversion is less reliable.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C11-3|MT016-C11-3]] — Warsh is the good cop appearing independent to protect dollar credibility while Bessent is the bad cop pushing printing and stock buying; Fed independence is shrinking.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C12-1|MT016-C12-1]] — Sequence: the Treasury buys back its own debt first, then in a crisis the Fed backstops the long end of the curve.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C12-2|MT016-C12-2]] — The plan routes equity purchases through a Treasury sovereign wealth fund funded by borrowing and printing, not direct central-bank stock buying, because it looks fairer.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C12-X1|MT016-C12-X1]] — Caveat: the Fed backstop requires a majority of the FOMC, not just the chair, so the speed and certainty of execution are uncertain.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C13-3|MT016-C13-3]] — Election outcomes matter more in populist periods and especially this midterm, since losing both houses would stop the plan, so the administration will do all it can to hold the Senate.
- [[MT016 - America's 250-Year Reset feat. Cem Karsan - Systematic Investor - Ep. 407#^MT016-C14-2|MT016-C14-2]] — The administration may want a controlled crisis post-midterm to take markets down and then rally them (take it down to take it up).

## [[MT034 - Christine Lagarde - President of the European Central Bank - Podcast - In Good Company]]
- [[MT034 - Christine Lagarde - President of the European Central Bank - Podcast - In Good Company#^MT034-C02-4|MT034-C02-4]] — The Middle East shock revived the European green transition and nuclear as a long-term priority to cut fossil energy dependence.

## [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294]]
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C02-3|MT036-C02-3]] — The Fed has committed to a rate-cut-hope narrative and is struggling when each inflation print comes in hot, as it did with the transitory narrative, so policy may not follow the data.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C02-4|MT036-C02-4]] — Powell argued there is no stagflation because growth is at least 3% and inflation below 3%.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C03-1|MT036-C03-1]] — Diverging central bank policies create trends in currencies, a sector that had not moved for a while, from which trend followers benefited (yen, Mexican peso).
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C08-3|MT036-C08-3]] — Use warehousing at low prices (avoiding a short trend position and roll costs), then convert to trend following once price rises to capture the full move.

## [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331]]
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C01-2|MT037-C01-2]] — The February 2025 German election could bring a policy change (e.g. fiscal) after a prolonged slump, though European policy normally moves slowly.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C05-3|MT037-C05-3]] — Since September 2024 the Fed cut by 1% while long-term US yields rose about 1%, a 'reverse conundrum'.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C06-1|MT037-C06-1]] — Fed funds futures moved about 1 to 1.25 points higher (year-end 2025 from 3% to 4%) as recession fears were priced out, explaining the rise in real yields.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C08-3|MT037-C08-3]] — A stronger yen on Bank of Japan rate-hike talk hurt Asian equities and trend followers.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C12-X1|MT037-C12-X1]] — The late-1990s rally continued for three years after the 'irrational exuberance' warning partly because the Fed kept policy easy (Y2K concerns, after the 1998 Asian/Russian wobble); whether today rhymes depends on whether the productivity story is as large as then.

## [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400]]
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C03-6|MT038-C03-6]] — Powell stepping down as Fed chair is flagged as a rate-relevant event, with some expecting lower rates.
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C03-7|MT038-C03-7]] — The 10-year JGB yield hit a 29-year high in May 2026.

## [[MT039 - Crowded Trades, Fluctuating Markets- Navigating the New Era of Investing - Systematic Inve]]
- [[MT039 - Crowded Trades, Fluctuating Markets- Navigating the New Era of Investing - Systematic Inve#^MT039-C03-5|MT039-C03-5]] — Markets are driven by one decision-maker's announcements and no model exists for that.
- [[MT039 - Crowded Trades, Fluctuating Markets- Navigating the New Era of Investing - Systematic Inve#^MT039-C04-1|MT039-C04-1]] — Presidential policy shocks (Nixon's 1971 dollar devaluation; Hoover's 1930 tariffs) were each followed by roughly a decade of no equity returns, raising the question whether current tariff policy could do the same.

## [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4]]
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C01-1|MT040-C01-1]] — In late July 2026 the US intervened in the yen, selling euro and buying yen, the first yen purchase since 1998, and said Japan could tap the Fed's FIMA facility to borrow dollars for further intervention.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C02-X1|MT040-C02-X1]] — The US sold euros to buy yen without informing the ECB, a sign of unilateral US policy and weaker international policy coordination.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C03-1|MT040-C03-1]] — In August 2026 the yen intervention and the US bond-market 'intervention' (Bessent's announcement) went against the prevailing trends and hurt trend followers.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C04-2|MT040-C04-2]] — The dollar sell-off after the bond intervention (with gold up) created a weaker-dollar reversal that hurt trend followers while currencies were choppy.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C05-1|MT040-C05-1]] — Fed chair Warsh blames QE for deficits, but shrinking the balance sheet would add bond supply while Treasury shifts issuance to bills, so large balance-sheet reduction is unlikely soon.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C05-2|MT040-C05-2]] — Alan expects little balance-sheet reduction near term, since the Fed chair speaks regularly with the president and has not given a framework.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C07-2|MT040-C07-2]] — Warsh's reduced guidance leaves the market without a sense of the Fed's reaction function, which is the problem rather than forward guidance itself.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C07-3|MT040-C07-3]] — The AI boom may be inflationary short term and disinflationary later, which complicates what policy is appropriate and how the Fed will respond.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C07-4|MT040-C07-4]] — Markets have always inferred the Fed's stance (liquidity matters), so telling markets to ignore the Fed makes no sense.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C08-1|MT040-C08-1]] — After the July FOMC press conference the curve steepened, the dollar fell and gold rose: classic signs of reduced Fed credibility, as the Fed has missed its inflation target for five years.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C08-2|MT040-C08-2]] — Three FOMC members favour raising rates, increasing the influence of Waller and Williams and raising the chance of factions within a politicised Fed.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C08-3|MT040-C08-3]] — Alan suspects Warsh is more dovish than he signals.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C08-X1|MT040-C08-X1]] — Warsh credited the market with doing the Fed's job via higher yields, but that rise itself reflected the market reading his previous press conference as hawkish, i.e. markets still trade the inferred Fed reaction function.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C08-X2|MT040-C08-X2]] — If the Fed chair keeps giving little guidance, FOMC minutes and the views of other members (Waller, Williams) become the more important signals of Fed policy.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C09-X1|MT040-C09-X1]] — Druckenmiller (Bessent's and Warsh's former colleague) argued in a WSJ op-ed that markets should set long rates and opposed the Treasury's bond-market intervention, an influential dissent from the administration's approach.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C10-2|MT040-C10-2]] — Debt sustainability scares recur (2023 issuance fears led Yellen to change refunding; now Bessent is tinkering), showing officials intervene when yields spike.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C11-3|MT040-C11-3]] — In the 2010s coordinated, successful central bank policies produced low, stable rates and inflation, a headwind for trend following.
- [[MT040 - Debt, Inflation & Fed Credibility- The Three Market Fractures - Systematic Investor - Ep.4#^MT040-C13-4|MT040-C13-4]] — Treasury acts when 10-year yields approach 5% (as in October 2023); a decisive break above could be the straw for equities and a strong environment for trend.

## [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner]]
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C04-3|MT041-C04-3]] — US export controls (no Blackwell, a 2-3x step over Hopper) widen the GPU gap, and DeepSeek has to use most of its small cluster for inference demand, which limits training.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C07-1|MT041-C07-1]] — Each new US restriction pushes China to invest more in open alternatives such as RISC-V.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C07-5|MT041-C07-5]] — OpenAI's CEO said OpenAI was on the wrong side of history on open source, which Gerstner reads as R1 forcing closed labs to open older models.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C08-2|MT041-C08-2]] — US policy should speed itself up (power generation and regulation) rather than only slow China, which Gerstner and Gurley call the constructivist camp view.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C08-X2|MT041-C08-X2]] — The same shock (R1) produced opposite policy demands in Washington, embrace open source versus lock down and regulate, so the policy response to a tech shock is hard to predict.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C10-2|MT041-C10-2]] — More US restrictions on China raise the probability of a Taiwan move, an unintended consequence.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C10-3|MT041-C10-3]] — The Feb 2025 tariff announcement (25% on Mexico/Canada, 10-15% on China) sold off markets overnight; a 30-day delay in exchange for border troops reversed it, and the market now expects the tariffs not to hit.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C10-X1|MT041-C10-X1]] — Pending Congressional proposals to ban use of R1 variants are a policy risk that could shut US users out of open models.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C11-2|MT041-C11-2]] — The market consensus (the Bessent view) treats tariffs as a threat used for tactical concessions, reinforced by Trump's record of extracting concessions from Colombia, Canada and Mexico.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C11-3|MT041-C11-3]] — Variant view: Trump sees tariffs as a permanent replacement for income tax (as before 1910), so tariffs may be discharged, not only threatened.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C13-3|MT041-C13-3]] — A tariff on Taiwan chips taxes Nvidia, AMD and buyers like Meta and Amazon because the US lacks advanced fabs, so it reduces chip purchases and AI research in the short run.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C13-4|MT041-C13-4]] — A delayed chip tariff conditioned on US fab construction works as a negotiating tactic that cuts dependence on Taiwan, unlike a permanent immediate tariff.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C14-1|MT041-C14-1]] — Because the tariff threat only works if the intent stays ambiguous, observers cannot tell negotiating tool from real policy.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C15-3|MT041-C15-3]] — Fiscal changes will go through reconciliation (no filibuster); the White House wants a bill by April or May 2025, which sets the timing for the budget impact.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C17-1|MT041-C17-1]] — The expected lawsuit under Article I's spending power is weak because the President can cite a duty to protect the country from debt crisis (impoundment doctrine).
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C17-2|MT041-C17-2]] — Either political pressure from DOGE transparency yields deeper cuts in reconciliation, or courts expand executive power over spending.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C19-1|MT041-C19-1]] — Arguing that DeepSeek had many GPUs invites tighter export controls, which would cut Nvidia revenue, so the bull argument can backfire.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C19-X1|MT041-C19-X1]] — Policy uncertainty dominates now (early 2025) but may clear by the second half of 2025 into 2026, after which the AI-driven bull case could resume.
- [[MT041 - DeepSeek, Open Source, Tariffs, DOGE, Market Impact - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Gerstner#^MT041-C20-1|MT041-C20-1]] — A sovereign fund could hold government equity stakes from rescues (as in GM), so bailouts should buy equity.

## [[MT042 - Diversification is more important than ever! - Systematic Investor - Ep.385]]
- [[MT042 - Diversification is more important than ever! - Systematic Investor - Ep.385#^MT042-C08-2|MT042-C08-2]] — Official data releases such as CPI and PCE act as focal points that confirm or deny themes for price trends, despite missing US data during the shutdown.
- [[MT042 - Diversification is more important than ever! - Systematic Investor - Ep.385#^MT042-C16-3|MT042-C16-3]] — Trade negotiations, balance and the dollar will shape macro themes over the next two years.

## [[MT044 - Drawdowns, Diversification, and the Hidden Risks in Today’s Markets - Systematic Investor]]
- [[MT044 - Drawdowns, Diversification, and the Hidden Risks in Today’s Markets - Systematic Investor#^MT044-C02-1|MT044-C02-1]] — The Fed plans to roll back a bank capital rule that limited Treasury holdings, which raises bank demand for Treasuries but sets up risk of another bank crisis.
- [[MT044 - Drawdowns, Diversification, and the Hidden Risks in Today’s Markets - Systematic Investor#^MT044-C02-2|MT044-C02-2]] — Capital adequacy rules can cause crises (Basel 2 contributed to 2008), so loosening or treating Bitcoin and stablecoins as bank assets is a risk Rob is concerned about.

## [[MT045 - Drawdowns, Recovery, and Replication - Systematic Investor - Ep.356]]
- [[MT045 - Drawdowns, Recovery, and Replication - Systematic Investor - Ep.356#^MT045-C02-X1|MT045-C02-X1]] — Staying outside the euro (Denmark) has worked better than joining because the country keeps control of its own interest rates, and the krone has stayed strong.
- [[MT045 - Drawdowns, Recovery, and Replication - Systematic Investor - Ep.356#^MT045-C03-1|MT045-C03-1]] — COMEX copper rose about 13% in a day on tariff news (largest since 1968) while London and Chinese copper were unaffected, and CTAs were likely on the right side.
- [[MT045 - Drawdowns, Recovery, and Replication - Systematic Investor - Ep.356#^MT045-C03-2|MT045-C03-2]] — Isolated tariff events can start trends: long signals in metals (copper, platinum) were building on trade tension and potential tariffs.
- [[MT045 - Drawdowns, Recovery, and Replication - Systematic Investor - Ep.356#^MT045-C07-2|MT045-C07-2]] — The two worst trend drawdown-recovery combinations coincided with Trump trade-war periods, 2018 and 2025.
- [[MT045 - Drawdowns, Recovery, and Replication - Systematic Investor - Ep.356#^MT045-C07-X1|MT045-C07-X1]] — Kaminski disputes that the 2015-2019 trend drawdown was mainly a Trump trade-war effect; the very low rate environment of that period was also a cause.
- [[MT045 - Drawdowns, Recovery, and Replication - Systematic Investor - Ep.356#^MT045-C08-2|MT045-C08-2]] — Tariff-driven positions (long copper) are fragile because tariffs can be reversed by the next statement.

## [[MT046 - Druckenmiller on How AI is Dominating His Long Portfolio]]
- [[MT046 - Druckenmiller on How AI is Dominating His Long Portfolio#^MT046-C03-3|MT046-C03-3]] — After the biggest, broadest asset bubble and a 500bp hike in one year, bankruptcies and bank failures like SVB are likely only the first of more to come because balance sheets built under free money break when rates jump.
- [[MT046 - Druckenmiller on How AI is Dominating His Long Portfolio#^MT046-C04-5|MT046-C04-5]] — The student loan payment pause supported consumption and its end in September removes that support.
- [[MT046 - Druckenmiller on How AI is Dominating His Long Portfolio#^MT046-C04-6|MT046-C04-6]] — The longer a hard landing is delayed, the higher the Fed's terminal rate and the stickier inflation becomes, which raises rather than lowers the odds of a hard landing.
- [[MT046 - Druckenmiller on How AI is Dominating His Long Portfolio#^MT046-C06-2|MT046-C06-2]] — Chinese stimulus will give a short sugar high of six to nine months, but long-term China will be an undynamic economy under Xi's control and not a challenge to US growth.

## [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340]]
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C01-1|MT047-C01-1]] — The March 2025 Fed projections cut GDP and raised inflation, showing a stagflationary bias even though the dots still imply two cuts.
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C01-2|MT047-C01-2]] — The FOMC projections' diffusion indices show nearly all participants see downside risk to growth, the most negative since late 2022/2023, and upside risk to unemployment.
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C01-3|MT047-C01-3]] — The inflation-risk diffusion index is as elevated as in 2021-22, so the Fed sees upside inflation risk as high as at the inflation spike.
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C02-1|MT047-C02-1]] — Tariffs create stagflation-like risk (slower growth, higher inflation); outcome depends on how long tariffs last and in what shape.
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C04-3|MT047-C04-3]] — The US faces a dollar trilemma: a weaker dollar helps manufacturing, a stable dollar preserves reserve-currency status, and the huge deficits need enough stable capital inflows to be funded.
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C05-1|MT047-C05-1]] — Miran's paper treats tariffs primarily as a negotiating tool and part of a package to correct dollar overvaluation, which has hurt manufacturing via the trade deficit.
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C05-2|MT047-C05-2]] — The 1985 Plaza Accord is the precedent for a coordinated agreement to weaken the dollar after overvaluation, now dubbed a Mar-a-Lago Accord.
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C05-3|MT047-C05-3]] — Proposals link the US security umbrella to FX and trade policy, requiring partners to pay by buying Treasuries or swapping holdings into century or perpetual bonds.
- [[MT047 - ETFs, Size, and the Commodity Problem - Systematic Investor 340#^MT047-C06-X1|MT047-C06-X1]] — A Mar-a-Lago-style accord (partners selling dollars and terming out Treasuries) is unlikely because it does not suit Europe or China and US leverage over them is doubtful; unilateral Fed money-printing to buy foreign currency could spiral into something dangerous.

## [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge]]
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C04-3|MT048-C04-3]] — Betting-market odds of a Harris win rose and the market grew more worried about tax increases; some estimate the Trump tax cuts contributed about 20% of S&P gains.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C06-4|MT048-C06-4]] — The Fed has often been late, so it may again be late to cut and avoid recession (it was late to raise rates in June 2021).
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C06-5|MT048-C06-5]] — The 10-year TIPS yield at 1.8% versus a neutral about 0.5%, with fed funds at 5.5% versus a 10-year at 4%, shows the Fed at a multi-decade high in restrictiveness.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C07-1|MT048-C07-1]] — Market-priced cuts of 125-150bp over four to five months merely return policy to neutral and are not a recession signal.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C07-2|MT048-C07-2]] — Chicago Fed's Goolsbee: with real rates at the cycle peak, no overheating risk and weakening data, policy restrictiveness is unjustified; inflation concern gives way to jobs.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C08-1|MT048-C08-1]] — Rate cuts made because of recession fear are often sold: markets fell after the Fed cut in early 2019 and again in 2001, so cuts do not by themselves protect equities.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C08-2|MT048-C08-2]] — In 2001 the Fed was behind the curve, started cutting after the dot-com bubble burst, and Nasdaq fell over 20% despite cuts.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C08-3|MT048-C08-3]] — Long yields have already fallen, lowering mortgage and variable-rate costs, so further Fed cuts have limited marginal effect; economic growth, unemployment and CPI matter more for year-end direction.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C13-4|MT048-C13-4]] — The BoJ reversed within 36 hours, saying it will not raise rates when markets are unstable, and the Nasdaq (QQQ) round-tripped from 475 to 450 and back; it was a long-tail event that can trigger a fuse when other risks exist.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C15-2|MT048-C15-2]] — A US court found Google illegally maintained its monopoly via the roughly $25bn per year Apple default deal; remedies are to be decided in September.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C21-2|MT048-C21-2]] — Non-standard acquihire/licence deals are primarily to avoid federal merger scrutiny, plus double-taxation risk exists, and regulators may close the loophole later.
- [[MT048 - Ep14. Public Market Volatility, AI Air Pocket, $GOOG Ruling - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad Ge#^MT048-C22-1|MT048-C22-1]] — The restrictive acquisition environment forces legal gymnastics; Brad hopes the next administration permits small acquisitions while policing true monopolies.

## [[MT049 - Episode 12- Bob Prince - Co-CIO of Bridgewater Associates]]
- [[MT049 - Episode 12- Bob Prince - Co-CIO of Bridgewater Associates#^MT049-C11-1|MT049-C11-1]] — After five years of imbalances (COVID, overheating, tight money), policy makers had restored balance across countries, but geopolitical chaos (tariffs, boycotts) now threatens it: a clash of stability vs instability.

## [[MT050 - Episode 15- Felix Zulauf - Founder of Zulauf Consulting]]
- [[MT050 - Episode 15- Felix Zulauf - Founder of Zulauf Consulting#^MT050-C05-3|MT050-C05-3]] — Business cycle analysis must adjust for distortions from authorities' interventions, so the cycle is less clean than in the 1960s-70s.
- [[MT050 - Episode 15- Felix Zulauf - Founder of Zulauf Consulting#^MT050-C05-6|MT050-C05-6]] — The key political-market variable in 2025 is trade policy, followed by capital flows.

## [[MT052 - Episode 28- Paul Krugman - Professor of Economics at The City University of New York]]
- [[MT052 - Episode 28- Paul Krugman - Professor of Economics at The City University of New York#^MT052-C03-X1|MT052-C03-X1]] — The most protectionist pre-2016 US episode was Reagan-era voluntary export restraints forced on Japanese autos, which still stayed inside the GATT-agreement framework rather than breaking it.
- [[MT052 - Episode 28- Paul Krugman - Professor of Economics at The City University of New York#^MT052-C04-1|MT052-C04-1]] — An underpowered fiscal response to the 2008 financial crisis (the Obama stimulus was too small) causes a slow recovery lasting years.
- [[MT052 - Episode 28- Paul Krugman - Professor of Economics at The City University of New York#^MT052-C04-2|MT052-C04-2]] — Fiscal austerity after the financial crisis in Europe deepened the damage to growth.
- [[MT052 - Episode 28- Paul Krugman - Professor of Economics at The City University of New York#^MT052-C04-3|MT052-C04-3]] — Under normal circumstances federal government policy has little macro impact, so the Fed decides macro outcomes; the Fed makes misjudgments but is open-minded and not stupid.
- [[MT052 - Episode 28- Paul Krugman - Professor of Economics at The City University of New York#^MT052-C07-1|MT052-C07-1]] — The US has lost credibility because everything since April 2nd 2025 (tariffs, e.g. 25% on South Korea despite a free trade agreement) violates binding trade agreements, so foreign partners have reason not to trust it.

## [[MT053 - Episode 4- Noah Yechiely - Former Head of Equities at Bridgewater Associates]]
- [[MT053 - Episode 4- Noah Yechiely - Former Head of Equities at Bridgewater Associates#^MT053-C06-X1|MT053-C06-X1]] — Politics and geopolitics (e.g. US-China relations and whether tariffs go higher or lower) affect every company and market, so even bottom-up investors must understand the macro and political forces acting on their holdings.

## [[MT054 - Episode 5- Jim Rogers - Legendary Investor & Co-Founder of Quantum Fund]]
- [[MT054 - Episode 5- Jim Rogers - Legendary Investor & Co-Founder of Quantum Fund#^MT054-C09-1|MT054-C09-1]] — Central banks printing money to solve problems is a temporary fix that rarely succeeds long term, because few countries become successful by debasing their currency.

## [[MT057 - Finding the Optimal Trend Following Rules - Systematic Investor 300]]
- [[MT057 - Finding the Optimal Trend Following Rules - Systematic Investor 300#^MT057-C02-1|MT057-C02-1]] — The Fed dot plot moved from several cuts to maybe one, and Niels thinks inflation is sticky (high for longer) and the next move might even be a hike, leaving markets without a clear path.

## [[MT058 - Five Forces Shaping the Global Economy - With Ray Dalio, Founder of Bridgewater Associates]]
- [[MT058 - Five Forces Shaping the Global Economy - With Ray Dalio, Founder of Bridgewater Associates#^MT058-C02-1|MT058-C02-1]] — When the US ended gold convertibility on Aug 15 1971 (and Roosevelt in 1933), a devaluation of money pushed equities up sharply, the opposite of what Dalio expected.
- [[MT058 - Five Forces Shaping the Global Economy - With Ray Dalio, Founder of Bridgewater Associates#^MT058-C04-1|MT058-C04-1]] — Dalio correctly called the 1982 EM debt default but wrongly predicted a depression; policy easing made Aug 1982 the exact equity bottom, so being right on events does not mean being right on market direction.
- [[MT058 - Five Forces Shaping the Global Economy - With Ray Dalio, Founder of Bridgewater Associates#^MT058-C06-4|MT058-C06-4]] — COVID-era governments sent out more money than the income losses, on top of negative interest rates and central bank bond buying, which pulled inflation up.
- [[MT058 - Five Forces Shaping the Global Economy - With Ray Dalio, Founder of Bridgewater Associates#^MT058-C07-2|MT058-C07-2]] — With old and new debt both unwanted, the central bank must choose between sharply higher rates, which hurt everything, or printing more money.
- [[MT058 - Five Forces Shaping the Global Economy - With Ray Dalio, Founder of Bridgewater Associates#^MT058-C09-X1|MT058-C09-X1]] — The one trigger both sides know would cause a war is the US backing Taiwan independence; election-period pledges to defend Taiwan and arms sales push toward that red line.
- [[MT058 - Five Forces Shaping the Global Economy - With Ray Dalio, Founder of Bridgewater Associates#^MT058-C14-X2|MT058-C14-X2]] — The host frames QE as having disabled the long end as a fiscal disciplinarian and sees rising yields as its return, raising the question of when Treasury yields pull liquidity from other assets.

## [[MT060 - Geoffrey Garrett - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management]]
- [[MT060 - Geoffrey Garrett - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT060-C10-3|MT060-C10-3]] — Norway reached about 90% EV share of new car sales through large subsidies, a charging network, taxi-lane access and no annual fees, so policy design rather than cold weather determined adoption; US cheap petrol makes the same logic harder.
- [[MT060 - Geoffrey Garrett - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT060-C11-1|MT060-C11-1]] — Germany faces a trade-off: protecting its auto industry against Chinese EVs risks retaliation that closes the Chinese market it needs, with VW considering plant closures.

## [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit]]
- [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit#^MT061-C04-2|MT061-C04-2]] — He framed Japan as a fork: reflation (Abenomics) or being crushed under debt, which justified a binary position.
- [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit#^MT061-C04-X1|MT061-C04-X1]] — The Bank of Japan controls yields only out to 10 years and lets the 30-year float, so stress in Japan's debt load shows up first in ultra-long JGB yields (the host notes 30-40 year yields jumping).
- [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit#^MT061-C10-3|MT061-C10-3]] — Rare-earth coercion makes US-China talks a hostage negotiation rather than a trade negotiation, so a positive economic relationship cannot be expected.
- [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit#^MT061-C12-1|MT061-C12-1]] — If China moves on Taiwan the US can hobble it financially via dollar access and sanctions on its four SOE and twelve joint stock banks.
- [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit#^MT061-C12-2|MT061-C12-2]] — Financial hobbling after a Taiwan attack would make it hard for Xi to hang on, so regime change is likely.
- [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit#^MT061-C13-1|MT061-C13-1]] — US policy turning from Saudi toward Iran was a blunder; Saudi and the UAE are principal US allies.
- [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit#^MT061-C13-2|MT061-C13-2]] — The UAE (G42) realigned with the US away from China, a positive development for US-Gulf ties and the Abraham Accords.
- [[MT061 - Global Macro Investing And Geoeconomics With Hedge Fund Investor Kyle Bass - Hoover Instit#^MT061-C13-3|MT061-C13-3]] — He thinks the October 7 attack aimed to derail the Abraham Accords, which he expects will still proceed.

## [[MT062 - Global Macro- Discretionary vs. Systematic - Who Wins- - Global Alts 2025 - Pablo Calderin]]
- [[MT062 - Global Macro- Discretionary vs. Systematic - Who Wins- - Global Alts 2025 - Pablo Calderin#^MT062-C02-6|MT062-C02-6]] — Geopolitical risk is two-sided for macro: elevated risk can be inflationary while its removal can be deflationary.
- [[MT062 - Global Macro- Discretionary vs. Systematic - Who Wins- - Global Alts 2025 - Pablo Calderin#^MT062-C02-8|MT062-C02-8]] — Policymakers may react to equity market levels (a Trump equivalent of the Fed put), so the new mantra is do not fight Trump.

## [[MT063 - Global Market Risks, Fertility Crisis & Navigating Uncertainty - Systematic Investor 324]]
- [[MT063 - Global Market Risks, Fertility Crisis & Navigating Uncertainty - Systematic Investor 324#^MT063-C01-X1|MT063-C01-X1]] — The November 2024 ruble collapse is read as the joint result of many interacting sanctions variables in a complex adaptive system, and it adds to geopolitical uncertainty (risk of desperate moves by Russia) because escalation is running on several fronts.
- [[MT063 - Global Market Risks, Fertility Crisis & Navigating Uncertainty - Systematic Investor 324#^MT063-C02-1|MT063-C02-1]] — A weaker ruble helps exports (energy sold in other currencies yields more rubles) but raises imported inflation, leaving the central bank with policy challenges when rates are already about 22%.
- [[MT063 - Global Market Risks, Fertility Crisis & Navigating Uncertainty - Systematic Investor 324#^MT063-C02-2|MT063-C02-2]] — US sanctions on Gazprom Bank were the probable catalyst for the ruble collapse in November 2024.
- [[MT063 - Global Market Risks, Fertility Crisis & Navigating Uncertainty - Systematic Investor 324#^MT063-C02-X1|MT063-C02-X1]] — If Western countries respect the sanctions there is little direct trade with Russia, so a ruble collapse should matter little to Western markets, and Russian energy sold to China and India may be priced in other currencies anyway.
- [[MT063 - Global Market Risks, Fertility Crisis & Navigating Uncertainty - Systematic Investor 324#^MT063-C02-X2|MT063-C02-X2]] — Public statements on Ukraine peace terms shifted within days (from 'on their terms' to accepting that Crimea will not return), which he reads as a sign that the Western side is preparing a negotiated settlement ahead of the change of US administration.
- [[MT063 - Global Market Risks, Fertility Crisis & Navigating Uncertainty - Systematic Investor 324#^MT063-C03-X1|MT063-C03-X1]] — Ukraine's hold on part of Russia's Kursk region is a bargaining chip (Kursk for Crimea), so Russia is concentrating forces there, with North Korean troops, to retake it before a deal is struck under the incoming Trump administration.

## [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD]]
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C05-X1|MT064-C05-X1]] — The 20+ year Treasury index fell 32 basis points in February 2025 after hot inflation figures, which hurt long-duration trend positions, but was still slightly positive for the year.
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C21-2|MT064-C21-2]] — Tariffs increase inflation in the US and globally through retaliation.
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C21-3|MT064-C21-3]] — Mass deportations cut labor supply and lift wages, adding to inflation.
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C21-R4|MT064-C21-R4]] — Rob's analysis of Trump's economic consequences assumes Trump succeeds in doing what he has said and appears to be trying to do.
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C21-X1|MT064-C21-X1]] — Tariffs face the most pushback from Republicans and other people the president listens to, so tariff plans are the policy most likely to be diluted or reversed.
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C22-1|MT064-C22-1]] — Tariffs on integrated US-Canada auto supply chains would be inflationary.
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C22-3|MT064-C22-3]] — Stopping government payments could cause unemployment, supply and demand shocks and uncertainty.
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C22-4|MT064-C22-4]] — Net view: policy uncertainty hurts equities and inflation hurts bonds, which favors CTAs.
- [[MT064 - Gold, Futures Pricing & Market Trends - Systematic Investor 335 REUPLOAD#^MT064-C23-X1|MT064-C23-X1]] — Bonds and equities react to different drivers: bonds focus on inflation, which Trump policies clearly raise, while the effect of inflation on equities is ambiguous, so bonds are the more direct place for policy risk to show first.

## [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar]]
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C02-2|MT065-C02-2]] — Weak growth with higher inflation brings higher rates and weaker asset markets.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C02-4|MT065-C02-4]] — Low growth with high inflation and rising rates (the 1970s) is the worst combination for financial assets in real terms.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C03-2|MT065-C03-2]] — Central banks long called inflation transitory, giving up the language only at the start of 2022, so they were slow to react.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C03-3|MT065-C03-3]] — In 2022 the central bank priority shifted to a whatever-it-takes fight against inflation, accepting recession risk.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C03-4|MT065-C03-4]] — The shift in central bank stance, not inflation alone, caused the summer 2022 market sell-off as investors feared tightening and growth impact.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C03-X1|MT065-C03-X1]] — Years of zero interest rate policy misallocated capital and punished savers, and central banks treated inflation as yesterday's issue, which is why the later tightening is belated and abrupt.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C04-5|MT065-C04-5]] — Equity bear markets usually end when interest rates and inflation peak.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C05-3|MT065-C05-3]] — Cyclical bear markets arise late in an expansion when inflation forces the central bank to raise rates and investors expect downturn and falling profits.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C07-1|MT065-C07-1]] — Within value, cyclicals fell on recession worries while energy, mining and materials, which pass on costs, outperformed in the inflation shock.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C07-6|MT065-C07-6]] — The Fed's faster tightening created a large rate differential that attracts investors to the dollar.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C08-1|MT065-C08-1]] — The dollar should ease as other central banks raise rates and uncertainty fades, with no collapse and still strong in the short term.
- [[MT065 - Goldman Sachs’s Chief Global Equity Strategist, Peter Oppenheimer, Discusses Macro and Mar#^MT065-C08-4|MT065-C08-4]] — UK fiscal stimulus conflicting with Bank of England tightening spooked markets and sterling.

## [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It]]
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C01-1|MT066-C01-1]] — Macro opportunity set scales with the amount of central bank rate movement, because moves in policy rates drive FX and equity volatility.
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C01-3|MT066-C01-3]] — Divergence in central bank policy across regions (Europe, US, Japan) creates relative-value and FX trades that favour macro trading.
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C01-4|MT066-C01-4]] — If the Bank of Japan loosens its yield-curve band while dollar-yen is stretched near 146, there is large downside risk in USDJPY (a policy-change catalyst), whereas long dollar has paid so far.
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C01-X1|MT066-C01-X1]] — Data point: in the 2022 hiking year some macro managers were up 200% or more and Graham's funds roughly 20-50%, ahead of Bridgewater and Citadel, evidence that a regime of large central-bank moves pays macro strategies.
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C02-5|MT066-C02-5]] — View: market pricing of Fed easing by end-2023 is too early; inflation falls in 2023 but is not near 2% until 2025 if then.
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C02-X1|MT066-C02-X1]] — The opposing (Siegel) camp in the debate: a Fed trying to regain credibility lost by calling inflation transitory risks over-tightening too fast and causing avoidable economic damage (Tropin sides with the other camp).
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C03-1|MT066-C03-1]] — Credibility camp view: the Fed must over-tighten to re-anchor expectations, implying a recession whose timing and length are the open questions.
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C03-5|MT066-C03-5]] — BoJ governor transition in April 2023 is a catalyst for loosening yield curve control, triggering a yen reaction (intervention already signals discomfort with weakness).
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C03-X1|MT066-C03-X1]] — Discretionary macro has two parts: forecasting the economic data and, at least as important, forecasting each central bank's reaction function to that data, which is where cross-country divergences come from.
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C04-X1|MT066-C04-X1]] — View (Oct 2022): the front end prices a policy peak near 4.75% by year-end easing to about 4.25% by mid-2023, while the 10-year sits at 3.95%, leaving the curve inverted; he sees the long end, not the front end, as mispriced.
- [[MT066 - Graham Capital Management's Tropin on Why Macro is Killing It#^MT066-C04-X2|MT066-C04-X2]] — Real rates have risen a lot, as was necessary, and will keep rising, which supports further bond weakness.

## [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel]]
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C01-1|MT067-C01-1]] — Pre-pandemic, persistently low inflation let the Fed ease quickly and tighten slowly (2015-18 hikes every other meeting at 25bp), creating the fed put and well-telegraphed policy.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C02-3|MT067-C02-3]] — Central banks worldwide surprised on how aggressive they would be (e.g. 75bp hikes) after a year earlier still doing QE, so the policy debate shifted from taper to pace of tightening.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C02-5|MT067-C02-5]] — Example of early reading of Fed communications (Dec 2021 press conference hints at earlier balance sheet runoff while still doing QE) as an opportunity outside consensus.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C03-2|MT067-C03-2]] — Central banks now react to headline CPI and even the Michigan inflation expectations survey, so those releases are tradable policy triggers.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C04-3|MT067-C04-3]] — The Fed put still exists but its strike is far lower because inflation limits easing; buying dips on pivot hopes is the old playbook.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C04-R1|MT067-C04-R1]] — In 2022 the best trade has been long dollar, short rates (bonds), short equities.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C06-2|MT067-C06-2]] — European central banks face energy-driven inflation and second-round effects yet must tighten into recession, reversing the fed-put logic of accommodating downside growth risk.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C07-2|MT067-C07-2]] — Japan's inflation (30-year high at 3%), YCC testing, weak yen and intervention limits create trade opportunities, with BoJ leadership change due.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C07-X1|MT067-C07-X1]] — Japan's yield-curve control (buying large amounts of JGBs) conflicts with intervening to support the yen, so with inflation at a 30-year high and markets testing YCC, the policy mix looks unsustainable and is a trade opportunity.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C07-X2|MT067-C07-X2]] — View: central banks will tighten somewhat further than market pricing implies because inflation is sticky.
- [[MT067 - Graham's 2022 Virtual Investment Symposium- Discretionary Strategies Panel#^MT067-C10-R1|MT067-C10-R1]] — Because of inflation, a macro shock now has to be met by much tighter monetary policy instead of easing, which creates instability, volatility and opportunity for macro funds.

## [[MT068 - Greg Jensen - Co-CIO of Bridgewater Podcast In Good Company Norges Bank Investment Managem]]
- [[MT068 - Greg Jensen - Co-CIO of Bridgewater Podcast In Good Company Norges Bank Investment Managem#^MT068-C08-1|MT068-C08-1]] — Base case: US inflation about 3% for the next couple of years with Fed tolerant above 2.5%, versus breakeven near 2.25-2.5%.
- [[MT068 - Greg Jensen - Co-CIO of Bridgewater Podcast In Good Company Norges Bank Investment Managem#^MT068-C08-2|MT068-C08-2]] — Populist control of the central bank leading to lower real rates during an AI boom would be inflationary.
- [[MT068 - Greg Jensen - Co-CIO of Bridgewater Podcast In Good Company Norges Bank Investment Managem#^MT068-C09-X1|MT068-C09-X1]] — View: a new Fed chair aligned with the administration may be outvoted often next year, giving a divided Fed until more aligned governors are appointed, a policy-uncertainty source.

## [[MT069 - Hard Lessons- Stan Druckenmiller- Invest, then investigate]]
- [[MT069 - Hard Lessons- Stan Druckenmiller- Invest, then investigate#^MT069-C03-X2|MT069-C03-X2]] — A Fed that cuts rates into a booming, fiscally stimulated economy risks inflation taking off, especially with commodity prices rising, which is the scenario where short bonds pay.

## [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408]]
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C06-1|MT071-C06-1]] — New Fed chair Warsh dislikes forward guidance and wants markets to infer policy from data, shifting the Fed toward less communication.
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C06-2|MT071-C06-2]] — In the Greenspan era the Fed often validated moves markets had already priced from the data, which is the model Warsh wants to return to.
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C07-1|MT071-C07-1]] — Forward guidance was introduced (Bernanke, 2004) to steer long-term rates before QE, because the Fed controlled only short rates.
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C07-2|MT071-C07-2]] — A study found 68% of 5-year Treasury yield variance after Fed meetings came from the statement and only 12% from the rate decision, so markets are addicted to Fed communication.
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C07-X1|MT071-C07-X1]] — A second rationale for Fed transparency was that policy surprises create market volatility, so forward guidance was meant to reduce shock-driven volatility; after the GFC, committing to low rates for long became a core unconventional tool.
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C08-1|MT071-C08-1]] — Less forward guidance should mean more uncertainty and more volatility around Fed policy.
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C08-R2|MT071-C08-R2]] — Warsh looked hawkish at his first meeting, but when he spoke at a European conference he said inflation risks are coming down, which is why Alan doubts he is as hawkish as the market inferred.
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C09-1|MT071-C09-1]] — Warsh faces the choice of tightening against AI-driven demand despite expected future disinflation; Alan doubts he is as hawkish as the market inferred.
- [[MT071 - Has Trend Following Changed Forever- - Systematic Investor - Ep.408#^MT071-C15-1|MT071-C15-1]] — Post-2009 central bank involvement and changed participants may explain fast trend's decline instead of tick size.

## [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350]]
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C01-1|MT072-C01-1]] — Tariff announcements, pauses and court rulings make economic data unusually patchy, so reading the economy from hard data will be difficult for months.
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C01-2|MT072-C01-2]] — Front-loading before tariffs followed by a pause distorts durable goods orders (down 7% after +6-7% the previous month), so single prints mislead.
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C02-1|MT072-C02-1]] — Port of Los Angeles bookings rose less than expected during the 90-day tariff pause since production and shipping lead times exceed the pause.
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C03-4|MT072-C03-4]] — The BOJ's earlier bond purchases shifted losses from the private sector to the central bank, so the yield rise did not cause private-sector stress.
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C07-3|MT072-C07-3]] — A recession would pull yields down short-term as the Fed eases, as seen in the last 2-3 years where deficit worries fade on weak data.
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C07-5|MT072-C07-5]] — The next Fed chair when Powell's term ends in 2026 is a risk because a chair under administration influence (Nixon/Burns) could ease too much, pushing yields up.
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C07-X1|MT072-C07-X1]] — In the alternative scenario where the economy does not weaken much and near-term inflation rises, a Fed forced to respond becomes the trigger that pushes yields up in the near term.
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C09-3|MT072-C09-3]] — Policy power alternates between politicians and central banks; today politicians dominate, so inflation becomes a political choice.
- [[MT072 - Higher Yields Are Here to Stay - Systematic Investor - Ep.350#^MT072-C10-2|MT072-C10-2]] — Section 899 could tax foreign holders of US stocks, bonds and dividends, a risk to foreign demand for US assets.

## [[MT073 - How Alpha Hides in Plain Sight - Systematic Investor - Ep.362]]
- [[MT073 - How Alpha Hides in Plain Sight - Systematic Investor - Ep.362#^MT073-C04-1|MT073-C04-1]] — Policy shocks such as copper tariffs create dislocations between adjacent contract spreads because one contract falls before and one after the tariff.

## [[MT074 - How CTAs and Portable Alpha Could Reshape Investing in 2025 - Top Traders Unplugged 149]]
- [[MT074 - How CTAs and Portable Alpha Could Reshape Investing in 2025 - Top Traders Unplugged 149#^MT074-C10-1|MT074-C10-1]] — In early 2025 German fiscal expansion, US policy shifts and DeepSeek changed the outlook for Europe, US tech and Asia, creating sources of big macro trends.
- [[MT074 - How CTAs and Portable Alpha Could Reshape Investing in 2025 - Top Traders Unplugged 149#^MT074-C10-3|MT074-C10-3]] — During the zero-rate QE era currencies gave little trend following return because coordinated policy suppressed risk appetite.
- [[MT074 - How CTAs and Portable Alpha Could Reshape Investing in 2025 - Top Traders Unplugged 149#^MT074-C10-4|MT074-C10-4]] — When inflation built and monetary policies diverged across regions in 2021-22, currencies became the main driver of CTA returns for two to three years.
- [[MT074 - How CTAs and Portable Alpha Could Reshape Investing in 2025 - Top Traders Unplugged 149#^MT074-C10-5|MT074-C10-5]] — In 2025 fixed income markets decoupled (Treasuries, Bunds, JGBs) and China shifted to risk-on, creating breeding grounds for sustained macro divergence.
- [[MT074 - How CTAs and Portable Alpha Could Reshape Investing in 2025 - Top Traders Unplugged 149#^MT074-C10-7|MT074-C10-7]] — The March 2025 FOMC revised growth down with inflation still elevated, hinting at stagflation.

## [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293]]
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C02-2|MT076-C02-2]] — The yen hit a 34-year low with the BoJ holding rates, and the trend may have further to run.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C07-X2|MT076-C07-X2]] — The Treasury quarterly refunding announcement is a second scheduled liquidity event next to the Fed, and in an election year the Treasury will make it as accommodative as possible, supporting markets after it passes.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C08-1|MT076-C08-1]] — If, after deleveraging, price recovers above technical levels with positive momentum in the 1-15 May window, buy the dips; if not, risk rises (if-then path rule).
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C09-1|MT076-C09-1]] — If momentum holds and vol stays pinned through the dangerous window, get back long, since the pullback will be bought and highs retested.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C09-2|MT076-C09-2]] — 2024 should still be a positive year because the Fed and Treasury effectively put a floor under markets and would respond to declines with stimulus.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C09-X2|MT076-C09-X2]] — Even if hot inflation prevents new stimulus, policymakers will not withdraw liquidity, so there is no policy cap on the upside.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C10-4|MT076-C10-4]] — Downside trends reverse much faster than upside trends, perhaps because central banks step in with a put.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C12-2|MT076-C12-2]] — Rising markets stimulate the economy through wealth effects (including crypto), so equities can go higher without Fed rate cuts.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C13-1|MT076-C13-1]] — Higher rates act with a lag and merely sequester liquidity, so they matter medium to long term but little short term; negative effects will come eventually but can take a long time.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C13-X1|MT076-C13-X1]] — Rate effects are asymmetric: a sudden cut gives immediate stimulus through refinancing and moving, while rate rises do not cut liquidity right away because people simply stay put.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C14-1|MT076-C14-1]] — Election uncertainty keeps entities from acting until results are known, with stimulus coming into the election and unknown thereafter.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C14-2|MT076-C14-2]] — The Fed's choice of growth over inflation is political and the politicization is structural, likely worse under Trump, resulting in worse inflation and a bigger right tail for markets.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C14-3|MT076-C14-3]] — Beyond price stability and employment, the Fed is meant to control volatility and smooth cycles, implicitly putting a floor under markets.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C14-X1|MT076-C14-X1]] — The Fed has never been fully independent because it shares interests with the US government and must coordinate with it, so policy should be modelled as jointly Fed-Treasury.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C15-1|MT076-C15-1]] — Powell acts to preserve the Fed's independence, so structural incentives rather than conspiracy explain Fed behaviour.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C15-2|MT076-C15-2]] — Loss of Fed independence is an upside risk for markets short term but a bigger structural problem long term.
- [[MT076 - How Global Conflicts affect the Economy & 2024 Outlook - Systematic Investor 293#^MT076-C15-4|MT076-C15-4]] — A Trump presidency may initially hurt China with tariffs but ultimately lead to a US-China deal.

## [[MT077 - How to Act Rationally During a Financial Crisis with Scott Nations]]
- [[MT077 - How to Act Rationally During a Financial Crisis with Scott Nations#^MT077-C08-3|MT077-C08-3]] — Government antitrust action against dominant firms (Microsoft 2000, Standard Oil, bankers 1907) creates uncertainty that markets dislike and can fuel crashes.

## [[MT078 - Howard Marks - Co-founder of Oaktree - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment]]
- [[MT078 - Howard Marks - Co-founder of Oaktree - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment#^MT078-C05-2|MT078-C05-2]] — In December 2023 the consensus of six Fed cuts in 2024 was excessively optimistic 'Goldilocks thinking'; expectations later fell to two or three.
- [[MT078 - Howard Marks - Co-founder of Oaktree - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment#^MT078-C06-1|MT078-C06-1]] — Negative rates were administered by central banks, unsustainable in Marks' view, but in the short run don't fight the Fed.
- [[MT078 - Howard Marks - Co-founder of Oaktree - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment#^MT078-C06-2|MT078-C06-2]] — Market pricing of six cuts versus the Fed dot plot of three was a mistake from excess optimism.

## [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306]]
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C02-1|MT079-C02-1]] — Heavy Treasury bill issuance relative to coupons acts as de facto QE that offsets Fed tightening, which helps explain strong US growth, slow disinflation and easy financial conditions.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C03-1|MT079-C03-1]] — Treasury's rule of thumb is about 15% bills, but actual issuance has been much higher, shifting supply versus demand along the curve.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C03-3|MT079-C03-3]] — If macro calms again (inflation and rates controlled), trend followers may struggle as in the decade to 2019.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C05-3|MT079-C05-3]] — Central-bank-intervention markets (managed FX) that punish speculators justify lower exposure.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C07-X1|MT079-C07-X1]] — Commodities whose prices government commodity boards can manage (like cocoa) look like poor trend markets, yet such markets can still produce explosive trends after a supply dislocation, so managed pricing alone is not a reason to exclude them.

## [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337]]
