- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C01-1|MT080-C01-1]] — Divergence in monetary policy and inflation (Bank of Japan versus others) raises the question of what happens to the carry trade if the BoJ hikes.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C02-1|MT080-C02-1]] — The short JGB trade, the 'widow-maker', is finally paying trend followers after decades, with a BoJ policy shift as a potential driver.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C03-3|MT080-C03-3]] — Policymakers cannot force bond yields down if inflation rises, because yields reflect buyers' appetite.

## [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod]]
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C05-8|MT081-C05-8]] — Implemented tariff levels are below the Liberation Day worst case but far above pre-2025 levels.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C06-1|MT081-C06-1]] — Apocalyptic economic forecasts were tied to the April 2-9 tariff levels that were later cut, so economists were not wrong on their inputs but misjudged the president's willingness to back off.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C06-2|MT081-C06-2]] — Tariff policy was reversed after a bank warning of a meltdown, i.e. financial market stress limits how far policy goes.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C08-1|MT081-C08-1]] — The Fed operates within a political climate: it hiked 75bp in 2022 with political permission to fight inflation, because neither party pushed back.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C08-2|MT081-C08-2]] — Presidential pressure made a cut politically easy, and the Fed is a little tight now, so a cut may reflect pressure as much as data.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C08-3|MT081-C08-3]] — Volcker would not have cut with inflation at 3% against a 2% target and rising.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C08-4|MT081-C08-4]] — The Fed follows the path of least resistance, and the long end of the yield curve is where resistance to more cuts would appear.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C08-X1|MT081-C08-X1]] — Former Dallas Fed president Rob Kaplan insists political pressure is never discussed at FOMC meetings; Leeman disagrees in substance, saying the pressure plays in the room without being discussed and the Fed both creates and reacts to the political milieu.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C09-2|MT081-C09-2]] — The Treasury Secretary criticised QE as raising asset prices and inequality; Liesman and Eisman accept it raised asset prices but say the alternative was worse.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C09-3|MT081-C09-3]] — The Treasury Secretary's long public critique of the Fed signals unusual political pressure on Fed independence.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C09-X1|MT081-C09-X1]] — Bernanke arrived having already worked out (2002 helicopter-money speech) that the Fed has many tools to fight deflation even at zero rates, which made him suited to launch QE in the financial crisis.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C11-1|MT081-C11-1]] — Greenspan's predictable quarterly hikes let the Street game the system; less clarity might have been better.
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C13-1|MT081-C13-1]] — Reforms that look humiliating (Russia refusing a $1B unemployment fund) foster the backlash that opposed them.

## [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1]]
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C04-1|MT082-C04-1]] — Tariff worries lifted US COMEX futures prices of gold, silver, copper and platinum above London spot prices because imported metal would face tariffs.
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C04-X1|MT082-C04-X1]] — Copper's early-2025 rally (about 15%) is driven first by China not yet being hit with US tariffs, alongside tariff-driven dislocations in COMEX pricing.
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C05-1|MT082-C05-1]] — Silver carries higher tariff risk than gold because gold is quasi-monetary and much silver comes from Mexico.

## [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377]]
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C01-2|MT084-C01-2]] — A Hassett Fed chair would likely be more dovish, supporting the administration view of a productivity boom and disinflation.
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C02-1|MT084-C02-1]] — Closeness of the likely Fed chair to the administration raises doubt about Fed independence.
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C06-2|MT084-C06-2]] — The Fed is cutting while inflation sits above target and progress to 2% has stalled, with a split Fed.
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C07-2|MT084-C07-2]] — Markets are complacent about Fed cuts with inflation above target and tariff pass-through still ahead.
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C12-2|MT084-C12-2]] — After the 2021-22 inflation spike, sensitivity is higher and the risk of de-anchored expectations is greater.
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C12-X1|MT084-C12-X1]] — Central bank inflation-fighting credibility, hard won under Volcker and Greenspan, is being squandered by cutting with inflation above target; because inflation embedded in the 1970s was so hard to wring out, this complacency is a very big risk.

## [[MT085 - Inside the mind of a legendary investor - Stan Druckenmiller - Highlights - In Good Compan]]
- [[MT085 - Inside the mind of a legendary investor - Stan Druckenmiller - Highlights - In Good Compan#^MT085-C02-6|MT085-C02-6]] — 1992 pound trade: Germany needed higher rates and the UK lower, so the DM-pound link was unsustainable, and the Bundesbank head's FT comment that they should be unlinked was the catalyst; he went 100% long DM short pound.

## [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318]]
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C04-2|MT086-C04-2]] — Trend following needs extreme, disruptive, consistent moves; when monetary-policy expectations ebb and flow (yes/no/maybe data), rates and FX trend signals whipsaw.
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C04-3|MT086-C04-3]] — Over summer 2024 the market focus shifted from inflation to slowdown, and trend positions rotated defensively (commodities down, bonds up).
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C05-3|MT086-C05-3]] — The Fed's expected plan is a 12-18 month cutting cycle down to about 3-3.5%, still much higher than the previous low.
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C12-1|MT086-C12-1]] — Kaminski's market view (Oct 2024): the bigger risk to a soft-landing consensus is renewed inflation (CPI uptick, commodities rebounding, China stimulus, port closures), with rates paused and higher for longer, which could drive next year's trends.
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C12-2|MT086-C12-2]] — Niels expects China trade policy to continue whoever wins the 2024 US election, limiting election impact.

## [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352]]
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C16-2|MT087-C16-2]] — Baltas's 2025 macro read: falling growth with range-bound yields and inflation pointed to being out of equities, long gold or a curve steepener, themes that played out but were missed by fast systematic signals.

## [[MT088 - Is OpenAI the Achilles’ Heel of the US Economy- - The Weekly Wrap]]
- [[MT088 - Is OpenAI the Achilles’ Heel of the US Economy- - The Weekly Wrap#^MT088-C01-1|MT088-C01-1]] — The 10-year Treasury yield nearly hit 4.8% in the week of Sept 4 2026; at some yield level the equity market will correct, level unknown (he had thought 4.5% was the line and was wrong).
- [[MT088 - Is OpenAI the Achilles’ Heel of the US Economy- - The Weekly Wrap#^MT088-C01-4|MT088-C01-4]] — In the week of Sept 4 2026 renewed war pushed oil prices up and raised inflation fears.
- [[MT088 - Is OpenAI the Achilles’ Heel of the US Economy- - The Weekly Wrap#^MT088-C05-3|MT088-C05-3]] — Meta settled with state attorneys general over harm to young users and agreed to change its algorithms; a legal analyst says it caps future tort exposure and pressures TikTok and Snap.

## [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive]]
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C03-3|MT089-C03-3]] — 2010-2021 was quiet for macro because central banks eased and gave forward guidance that removed rate uncertainty and volatility.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C03-4|MT089-C03-4]] — July 2010 to July 2021 the ECB, Fed and BoE made about 1325bp of hike-equivalent moves in total (about 1.2 hikes per year), versus just under 50 25bp-equivalent hikes in 2022 alone: 30x the action.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C04-1|MT089-C04-1]] — Macro opportunity set depends on central bank activity and volatility in FX, rates, equities and commodities; high-activity regimes are favourable.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C07-5|MT089-C07-5]] — The dollar looked slightly weak to Tropin (EUR/USD trying to break above 1.10) as the ECB was seen more hawkish than the Fed after a 15-month strong dollar.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C08-1|MT089-C08-1]] — The 2s10s curve had been 100bp inverted and was about 60bp; he expected further steepening as the economy enters recession.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C08-3|MT089-C08-3]] — Markets priced 30-40bp of Fed cuts in late 2023; Tropin was not convinced the Fed could cut, a modest mispricing.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C08-4|MT089-C08-4]] — No 2008-style crisis expected: the Fed response to the bank runs was good, but risks (debt ceiling, Ukraine, politics) call for vigilance.

## [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next]]
- [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next#^MT090-C04-1|MT090-C04-1]] — Debate on 2026: inflation constrains a new Fed chair versus slowdown allowing cuts; Tropin is skeptical of more cuts. Japan is raising rates and Europe sits in between.
- [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next#^MT090-C04-X1|MT090-C04-X1]] — The US curve steepener stalled in late 2025 partly because of normal position churn and partly because the administration is pushing for lower rates, which leans against the term-premium repricing in the short run.
- [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next#^MT090-C05-2|MT090-C05-2]] — Markets can run beyond central banks (Japan, Europe, Canada turning hawkish), though in the long run central banks regain control.
- [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next#^MT090-C05-3|MT090-C05-3]] — US economy outside AI is slowing (deflationary), while tariffs act as a consumer tax companies pass on (car makers, EU goods at 15-20%), so inflation outcome is mixed.
- [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next#^MT090-C05-X1|MT090-C05-X1]] — Whether a Fed put protects equities in an AI correction is uncertain because the likely next chair (Hassett) is an outsider, so the committee's cohesion and reaction function are unknown.
- [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next#^MT090-C06-4|MT090-C06-4]] — Japan is driving global rates higher (BoJ hawkish for the first time in decades), as are Canada and Europe, despite weak economies, a unprecedented pattern.
- [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next#^MT090-C07-1|MT090-C07-1]] — FX rule: the most hawkish central bank has the strongest currency; when all are hawkish the dollar trades in a range (6 months), so take little risk either way.

## [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management]]
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C02-1|MT091-C02-1]] — A year ahead the US will still have tariffs on Europe of roughly 10-20%, with European retaliation and a lot of noise.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C02-2|MT091-C02-2]] — Broad 20% tariffs act like a large tax increase, reduce trade and disrupt supply chains.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C02-3|MT091-C02-3]] — The net effect of tariffs depends on whether they are paired with offsetting supply-side reforms such as deregulation.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C02-X1|MT091-C02-X1]] — Tariffs are politically popular with the American public (the caption's 'terrorists' is 'tariffs'; even his well-educated mother liked them), so economists' objections have little political traction and tariffs are less likely to be dropped.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C04-3|MT091-C04-3]] — Do not rely on a presidential put: the president is convinced tariffs are good, so markets should not assume a quick policy reversal after drawdowns.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C06-X1|MT091-C06-X1]] — China is weighing a move on Taiwan and has studied the sanctions imposed on Russia, which is why it wants renminbi pricing and its own clearing rails as protection against US financial sanctions.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C08-3|MT091-C08-3]] — Inflation is a political economy problem; without real central bank independence a country periodically runs into inflation trouble.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C08-5|MT091-C08-5]] — In the next shock central banks will prioritize avoiding recession over fighting inflation because political pressure is stronger than in 2008-2009.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C09-3|MT091-C09-3]] — Political appointment of dovish central bankers is a path from debt to inflation.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C09-4|MT091-C09-4]] — The Fed lacks constitutional protection like the ECB, and pressure on its independence comes from both left and right, so independence could disappear quickly.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C09-6|MT091-C09-6]] — In a climate shock met by large spending, policymakers would likely tolerate inflation rather than raise rates.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C09-8|MT091-C09-8]] — Opponents argue independence is no longer needed because inflation is low, but inflation is low because of independence.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C10-2|MT091-C10-2]] — Expect Nixon-style price controls and other controls in response to inflation, rather than waiting for the fourth year of the term.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C10-5|MT091-C10-5]] — The Fed, if left independent, would raise rates if inflation rose even with strong jobs, setting up a showdown with politicians.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C10-X1|MT091-C10-X1]] — Sequence of political responses to inflation: first the executive reaches for tools such as price controls and financial repression, and only later does a direct fight over Fed independence arrive.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C10-X2|MT091-C10-X2]] — Even with strong jobs, high interest rates have losers (young people buying houses and cars), which creates political pressure on the Fed and makes a showdown likely when it hikes against inflation.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C11-1|MT091-C11-1]] — The Fed should keep to inflation; academic economists leaning toward climate, inequality and social justice are naive, and Congress should handle those goals.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C11-2|MT091-C11-2]] — Dot plots and extended forward guidance are overdone: markets discount them and later they constrain the central bank.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C11-3|MT091-C11-3]] — Central banks were slow to hike in 2021-22 because conditions looked like they could get really bad and they wanted to be cautious.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C12-1|MT091-C12-1]] — The supply-chain explanation fails because prices did not fall when supply chains normalized, so the 2021-22 inflation was a central bank mistake.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C12-2|MT091-C12-2]] — Central banks should have raised rates sooner rather than look through a big shock as temporary.

## [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast]]
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C02-4|MT092-C02-4]] — Tariff-driven inflation worries can limit the Fed's ability to ease, so cuts may disappoint.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C03-1|MT092-C03-1]] — Trump's tariff numbers are an opening negotiating stance, then he compromises and obtains favorable deals (e.g. Europe), consistent with his prior business conduct.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C03-2|MT092-C03-2]] — The Fed is divided: some see tariffs as slowing growth and want cuts, others see them inflationary and refuse to cut.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C03-3|MT092-C03-3]] — Fed cut expectations for the year collapsed from four to about one after the tariff announcements.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C03-X2|MT092-C03-X2]] — The ECB delivered the rate cuts markets expected while the Fed disappointed (four cuts priced at the start of the year, about one now), a policy divergence driven by US tariff-inflation worries.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C06-1|MT092-C06-1]] — Pressure on the Fed and its independence upsets markets and causes small price corrections, but he expects it to fade and a more accommodative chair to replace Powell.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C06-2|MT092-C06-2]] — Trump will appoint a capable but accommodative successor to Powell.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C07-2|MT092-C07-2]] — The market prices a Fed cut by year end and he expects the chair to cut in the last third of the year.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C07-X2|MT092-C07-X2]] — The Fed faces an unprecedented unknown, whether tariffs prove inflationary or slow the consumer and economy, and he thinks the surrounding dialogue about the Fed is overdone and the Fed is doing its best.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C07-X3|MT092-C07-X3]] — Ken links the expected Fed cut in the last third of the year to political pressure on Powell, i.e. the Fed's easing timing is partly driven by the threat to its chair and independence.

## [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -]]
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C02-3|MT093-C02-3]] — At the September 2025 25bp Fed cut, new governor Miran dissented for 50bp but contender Waller did not, and Powell dodged a question about Fed independence.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C02-X1|MT093-C02-X1]] — After the 25bp September cut, Waller (a contender for Fed chair) did not dissent and Powell did not signal capitulation to a run of cuts, which the administration would not have wanted to see; the Fed is not pre-committed to many cuts.

## [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2]]
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C01-1|MT094-C01-1]] — The administration's tariff push is a philosophical, doctrinal belief about fair trade rather than just a bargaining ploy; the debate is only how big, so conservative positioning was warranted.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C02-1|MT094-C02-1]] — The pro-growth agenda (lower taxes, less regulation, M&A flywheel, AI super cycle) is the bullish side; the open question is how large tariffs land and how budget cuts offset.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C02-3|MT094-C02-3]] — Reciprocal tariffs are calculated from effective rates that include non-tariff barriers (currency manipulation, judicial actions), so the numbers can be set arbitrarily.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C02-X1|MT094-C02-X1]] — The market's key question before Liberation Day was where tariffs would land between the hawk camp (Navarro, Lutnik: $600bn to $1tn) and the lower-end camp (Bessent, Hassett); the landing point and the budget-cut glide path determine whether the pro-growth agenda dominates.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C03-1|MT094-C03-1]] — Tariff structure: autos 25%, country-specific reciprocal rates, and a minimum 10% on all countries, with China a separate negotiation.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C03-2|MT094-C03-2]] — The announced tariff table is a starting point for ad hoc country-by-country negotiations (e.g. Vietnam) before April 9.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C03-3|MT094-C03-3]] — Tariff revenue would rise from about 77bn to 750bn, above Navarro's 600bn estimate, but pharma and semiconductor exemptions probably bring it to about 600bn.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C05-3|MT094-C05-3]] — Tariffs will settle closer to 300-400bn because the president wants deals and congressional Republicans, hearing from CEOs, constrain him (he cannot lose a vote on the reconciliation bill).
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C05-X1|MT094-C05-X1]] — Bill sees many negative ramifications of the tariff package (trading partners retaliating, realigning with China) and nothing in it that is good for markets or stocks, though he says macro is outside his usual world.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C06-1|MT094-C06-1]] — No clearing event for tariffs for at least 30-60 days, so the market remains in the fog of war.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C06-3|MT094-C06-3]] — A US-China trade deal is imminent because tariffs of this size on China are too large to hold.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C08-3|MT094-C08-3]] — Washington wants the world to run on US compute and models (Llama, OpenAI) rather than DeepSeek on Huawei chips, tying to AI diffusion restrictions.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C18-3|MT094-C18-3]] — TikTok US rumored structure: new company with ByteDance under 20%, existing ByteDance shareholders (60% US investors) and about half new investors; the licence is to the algorithm.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C18-X1|MT094-C18-X1]] — The TikTok sale, which needed extension by April 5, will be settled inside the broader US-China tariff negotiation rather than separately.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C19-2|MT094-C19-2]] — The TikTok US valuation may be low because Trump negotiates (sovereign wealth fund mention) and must clear Oracle audit of the algorithm.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C20-1|MT094-C20-1]] — After 54% tariffs, China could block the TikTok deal, so an announced deal is not done.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C20-2|MT094-C20-2]] — 54% tariffs on China will not stay because cooperation is too important (Ukraine, Middle East) and the president likes dealmaking with Xi.

## [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369]]
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C05-3|MT095-C05-3]] — Dallas Fed's Logan says the Fed needs a new operating target beyond Fed funds because ample reserves mean the market has shrunk and no longer represents money market rates.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C05-X1|MT095-C05-X1]] — Uncertainty about Fed independence is one of the reasons offered for the gold rally, by lowering confidence in dollar and Treasury safety.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C06-1|MT095-C06-1]] — The Fed funds market trades only about 100bn a day while tri-party repo trades in trillions, so repo or SOFR is a candidate target.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C06-2|MT095-C06-2]] — A repo-based target is more volatile but deeper and clearer, so it should reduce uncertainty; the target drives margin and collateral costs across markets.

## [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355]]
- [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355#^MT096-C05-1|MT096-C05-1]] — A Trump-appointed dovish Fed chair, even as one vote, could tilt the FOMC toward cuts, which would be negative for the long end of the bond market via inflation/term-premium risk.

## [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)]]
- [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)#^MT098-C18-1|MT098-C18-1]] — Quantitative easing is less effective than assumed because the central bank sets the quantity of long bonds, not the price, whereas at the short end it sets the overnight price as a monopolist; yield curve control would set long yields explicitly.
- [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)#^MT098-C18-2|MT098-C18-2]] — An inverted yield curve (10-year below 2-year) signals that the market expects lower long-term inflation than the Fed and that policy is too tight, which is why the curve predicts recessions.
- [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)#^MT098-C18-X1|MT098-C18-X1]] — Long yields float around expectations of where the Fed will take the short rate (dog on a leash); if the Fed announced a target such as 10-year at 3% it would be reached within days since no one can arbitrage the reserve monopolist, but yield curve control would sacrifice the market signal the free long end provides.
- [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)#^MT098-C19-1|MT098-C19-1]] — The Fed's third mandate is financial stability via payment-system clearing; before the Fed, private clearing houses broke down in panics (1907), and central banks are good at resolving them as in 2008.
- [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)#^MT098-C20-1|MT098-C20-1]] — Roche opposes QE and discretionary 'fingers in the air' rate setting, preferring systematic rules.

## [[MT099 - Major Market Moves for Trend Followers - Systematic Investor 308]]
- [[MT099 - Major Market Moves for Trend Followers - Systematic Investor 308#^MT099-C02-3|MT099-C02-3]] — The BOJ stated publicly it would not hike if it upsets markets, effectively a BOJ put that enabled the rebound, after a small BOJ hike and weak US jobs triggered the biggest Nikkei daily loss since 1987.

## [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag]]
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C01-1|MT100-C01-1]] — The EU Digital Markets Act aims to keep markets open to startups and scale-ups so success depends on ideas and capital rather than gatekeeper access, framed as regulation that keeps markets healthy and leaves room for innovation.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C02-1|MT100-C02-1]] — EU merger control prefers remedies (divestitures) to prohibition and rarely blocks deals; remedy buyers are preferred if in the same industry rather than a venture fund because later buyer failure harms customers and workers.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C02-2|MT100-C02-2]] — Regulation made quickly and not fully implemented underperforms; thoughtful, consulted, resourced legislation solves proven problems in the lightest manner, and bureaucratic burden is heavy in some sectors.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C03-2|MT100-C03-2]] — EU regulation targets use cases of AI (e.g. health systems recognizing heart attack symptoms in women) rather than the technology, because the harm appears where people are discriminated in use.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C04-1|MT100-C04-1]] — The Digital Services Act requires systems to filter content illegal under national law, notify users and allow complaint, and requires risk assessments (e.g. addictive design); TikTok cancelled a points-for-engagement product in Europe because it could not certify a risk assessment.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C04-2|MT100-C04-2]] — Breaking up big tech is a last resort, though the Google ad tech case (Google owns the buy side, sell side and marketplace) gave a conflict of interest that the Commission said could hardly be solved without third-party governance of part of it.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C05-3|MT100-C05-3]] — Biotech needs its own EU act because it is regulated like old chemicals, slowing innovation-to-market time; faster regulation is a competitiveness factor.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C05-X1|MT100-C05-X1]] — Big-tech threats to stop launching products in Europe because of regulation are taken as a signal that Europe must raise competitiveness; Vestager agrees more regulation does not improve competitiveness, except where tailored rules (e.g. a biotech act) shorten time to market.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C06-1|MT100-C06-1]] — The AI Act does not restrict AI research; it requires measures only where use risks discrimination, and trustworthy AI in welfare, health and education can be a market-creating selling point abroad.
- [[MT100 - Margrethe Vestager - Commissioner for Competition in the EU - Norges Bank Investment Manag#^MT100-C09-1|MT100-C09-1]] — Vestager worries that gigantic firms disregarding the legitimacy of rules would threaten democratic order.

## [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322]]
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C03-2|MT101-C03-2]] — Lower taxes or lighter regulation favour small caps over large caps because fixed regulatory costs weigh more on small firms; Russell 2000 outperformed after the election.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C03-3|MT101-C03-3]] — Fed cut 25bp but long yields kept rising, breaking the classic easing-lowers-yields relation; long end trend continued.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C03-4|MT101-C03-4]] — The Fed's average inflation targeting framework has effectively been abandoned.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C03-X5|MT101-C03-X5]] — Powell's comments dampened expectations of further Fed cuts in December 2024 because the economy is strong.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C04-2|MT101-C04-2]] — Rising TIPS breakevens can influence the Fed, so the bond market constrains the Fed as well as fiscal policy.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C05-X7|MT101-C05-X7]] — After the election, markets trade on strong high-frequency data and on broad expected policies (tax cuts, deregulation, tariffs, deportations) whose details are not yet known, which drives small-cap moves.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C06-3|MT101-C06-3]] — Swap spreads falling reflect expectations that Treasuries get lighter bank capital treatment (supplementary leverage ratio relief) to support demand for bonds.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C08-X12|MT101-C08-X12]] — Bond volatility (MOVE) has stayed high relative to equity volatility (VIX) because of inflation and uncertainty over Fed pivots.

## [[MT102 - Market Patterns Work and Jeffrey Hirsch Explains Why - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep 56]]
- [[MT102 - Market Patterns Work and Jeffrey Hirsch Explains Why - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep 56#^MT102-C12-X14|MT102-C12-X14]] — Under Trump, deals and surprise announcements tend to come over weekends and hit markets on Monday ('taco Tuesday'), so weekend gap risk is higher.

## [[MT103 - Market Predictions, Rates & Inflation, DOGE, CES, AI Compute - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad G]]
- [[MT103 - Market Predictions, Rates & Inflation, DOGE, CES, AI Compute - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad G#^MT103-C03-1|MT103-C03-1]] — The 10-year yield rose almost 100bp while the Fed was cutting, the opposite of the housing and economic tailwind expected.
- [[MT103 - Market Predictions, Rates & Inflation, DOGE, CES, AI Compute - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad G#^MT103-C12-2|MT103-C12-2]] — 10-year about 4.7% and market pricing of cuts fell to one; Trump tax cuts (400bn annual stimulus) and deregulation raise inflation worries even as core PCE falls (shelter), so rates stay higher.
- [[MT103 - Market Predictions, Rates & Inflation, DOGE, CES, AI Compute - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad G#^MT103-C15-2|MT103-C15-2]] — AI regulation risk moved to states (about 25 initiatives, Texas bill); a state patchwork would hand China an advantage and favour regulatory capture.

## [[MT104 - Market Structure, Liquidity, and Reflexivity in 2025 - Systematic Investor - Ep.368]]
- [[MT104 - Market Structure, Liquidity, and Reflexivity in 2025 - Systematic Investor - Ep.368#^MT104-C01-X1|MT104-C01-X1]] — Governmental and institutional changes are ignored by markets until they have structural effects, and Jem expects them to accelerate into the 2026 midterms and become a market focus.
- [[MT104 - Market Structure, Liquidity, and Reflexivity in 2025 - Systematic Investor - Ep.368#^MT104-C08-3|MT104-C08-3]] — Inflation is the ultimate check on liquidity-driven markets because government cannot control it, especially when the Fed abandons rules-based smoothing.
- [[MT104 - Market Structure, Liquidity, and Reflexivity in 2025 - Systematic Investor - Ep.368#^MT104-C08-R1|MT104-C08-R1]] — An independent Fed would normally react to inflation or an overextended market by withdrawing liquidity, which usually triggers the end of a bubble; its absence removes that brake.
- [[MT104 - Market Structure, Liquidity, and Reflexivity in 2025 - Systematic Investor - Ep.368#^MT104-C09-2|MT104-C09-2]] — Persistent negative real rates drive leveraged borrowing into appreciating assets and feed inflation (1970s); best hedges are non-inflation-controlled assets: precious metals, crypto, debasement trades, not breakevens.
- [[MT104 - Market Structure, Liquidity, and Reflexivity in 2025 - Systematic Investor - Ep.368#^MT104-C09-3|MT104-C09-3]] — Short term, an incentivized administration controls asset reflation into midterms; this lasts until inflation breaks it.
- [[MT104 - Market Structure, Liquidity, and Reflexivity in 2025 - Systematic Investor - Ep.368#^MT104-C15-X2|MT104-C15-X2]] — A calendar event risk for the end of 2025: Supreme Court arguments on the tariffs in November with a ruling afterwards.
- [[MT104 - Market Structure, Liquidity, and Reflexivity in 2025 - Systematic Investor - Ep.368#^MT104-C16-X1|MT104-C16-X1]] — Full political control of the Fed arrives in May (end of Powell's term) and markets already know it, so expected liquidity is on the way while institutions are caught underweight.

## [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371]]
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C04-X3|MT105-C04-X3]] — A policy shock (new US sanctions on Russian oil companies) lifted crude about 5% intraday, an example of an exogenous event that can start an energy trend.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C06-1|MT105-C06-1]] — The stability of inflation (not only its level) strongly shapes trending opportunities; central banks succeeded in keeping inflation stable, producing compression.

## [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39]]
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C02-1|MT106-C02-1]] — Equity markets showed a muted reaction to a large oil supply shock from the war, which Carver expects to matter eventually.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C04-2|MT106-C04-2]] — Treasury Secretary Bessent said the Fed should wait and see on rate cuts during the Iranian war, giving mixed signals with the White House.

## [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333]]
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C02-2|MT107-C02-2]] — Diverging central bank policy (BOJ hiking, ECB cutting, Fed on hold) creates market divergence and manager dispersion that could suit trend models.

## [[MT109 - Mike Pompeo, Former US Secretary of State & CIA Director, on Geopolitics, Leadership & the]]
- [[MT109 - Mike Pompeo, Former US Secretary of State & CIA Director, on Geopolitics, Leadership & the#^MT109-C03-X2|MT109-C03-X2]] — Policy view: the US should supply Ukraine with weapons and materiel (which it is good at producing) rather than troops, since Ukrainians asked for equipment only; this keeps America safe by blocking Putin.

## [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep]]
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C02-1|MT110-C02-1]] — Individual central bankers matter at key moments, e.g. Greenspan in 1994 hurt mortgage portfolios.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C02-2|MT110-C02-2]] — Greenspan's cuts to about 1 percent fed the housing bubble that led to the financial crisis.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C02-3|MT110-C02-3]] — The surprise first rate hike in about 5 years in 1994 was initially bad for trend followers.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C02-4|MT110-C02-4]] — Barclays/Bloomberg data: S&P fell in the first 3 months of each new Fed chair (Greenspan -33%, Powell -7%, Yellen -4%, Bernanke -2%).
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C02-X1|MT110-C02-X1]] — Fed communication went from a statement of a couple of hundred words in 1994 to meeting notes, projections and press conferences; the new chair is moving back toward minimal communication and no forecasts, a reversal of the transparency regime.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C03-1|MT110-C03-1]] — Hormuz normalisation took Brent to 4-month lows, a regime change in energy that pressured June 2026 trend-following performance.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C06-2|MT110-C06-2]] — Structural regime changes (a new Fed chair stamping policy) can create new trends or reversals, as opposed to behavioural responses to shocks.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C07-2|MT110-C07-2]] — Chair Warsh was expected to be dovish and less independent, but Mark thinks he is less dovish.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C07-3|MT110-C07-3]] — In practice the Fed runs a 3-4 percent inflation range rather than 2 percent: hikes near 4 and cuts below 3.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C07-4|MT110-C07-4]] — Fed politics has three layers: executive versus Fed, Wall Street versus Main Street, and internal dissent.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C08-1|MT110-C08-1]] — Warsh gives no dot and signals dot plots are in pencil, so forward guidance is likely cut back.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C08-4|MT110-C08-4]] — An AI productivity boom could allow looser monetary policy, as Greenspan did in the 1990s.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C08-X1|MT110-C08-X1]] — A fourth layer of Fed politics is the academics, whose models validate whatever the central bank does; this validation shows internal consistency, not that the policy is good, so academic support is weak evidence of sound policy.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C09-2|MT110-C09-2]] — Less Fed speech makes prices less noisy and driven by fundamentals, benefiting trend following.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C09-X3|MT110-C09-X3]] — Forward guidance was useful at the zero lower bound but now actions should drive prices, so diminishing its importance removes speech-driven noise from market prices.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C10-4|MT110-C10-4]] — Inflation has been above 2 percent for 5+ years though Bernanke and Yellen feared deflation.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C11-2|MT110-C11-2]] — The key H2 2026 regime change is monetary policy under the new chair, though not immediately visible in P&L; early months could be negative.

## [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336]]
- [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336#^MT111-C03-2|MT111-C03-2]] — The widow-maker short JGB trade (previously punished by BoJ intervention) began paying for trend followers once JGB 10y yield rose from -0.25% to 1.44% since Sept 2024, a policy regime shift.
- [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336#^MT111-C04-3|MT111-C04-3]] — Swiss interest-rate swaps, untradable via futures, allowed trend capture of SNB sub-zero rate prospects, an example of alternative markets adding return sources.

## [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep]]
- [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT112-C10-2|MT112-C10-2]] — 2025 sell-offs were reversals without follow-through, like March 2020, where central bank stepped in and trend followers positioned for a further sell-off that never came.
- [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT112-C10-3|MT112-C10-3]] — Man-made crises (lockdowns, tariffs) can be turned on and off quickly; trend following needs a slow behavioural response from investors that fast policy responses prevent.
- [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT112-C10-X1|MT112-C10-X1]] — When policy can swing quickly either way, the market setting rewards owning convexity (long gamma) rather than holding directional positions, because the size and sign of the next policy move cannot be predicted.
- [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT112-C11-1|MT112-C11-1]] — Political and central bank intervention (stimulus, tariff pauses, calls to buy) prevents follow-through in markets and makes trend environments difficult.

## [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini]]
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C01-1|MT113-C01-1]] — For ten years after the GFC every shock was met by very accommodative central banks (zero lower bound, sticky-low inflation), a special regime that is over after the 2022 inflation shock.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C01-2|MT113-C01-2]] — The inflation shock must be met by a shock to real rates, so asset markets and central banks play a completely different game from the post-GFC decade.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C02-3|MT113-C02-3]] — The 2022-23 hiking cycle is a record in magnitude and speed: double the average of previous four cycles (about 220bp) in half the time, heading to about 500bp.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C03-X1|MT113-C03-X1]] — The 2022 hiking shock was a Fed communication failure: in past cycles good forward guidance (or good market reading) left markets resilient, whereas in 2022 the Fed hiked far beyond what it had guided, producing a larger shock to asset prices.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C04-1|MT113-C04-1]] — Core inflation is persistent (rises stay) so it needs a larger policy response and causes a larger asset price reaction than energy inflation.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C07-1|MT113-C07-1]] — Short equities: earnings must fall and multiples are too high; either sticky core inflation forces the Fed to hike versus priced cuts, or core falls only via unemployment spike and recession cutting earnings; views are changeable.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C07-2|MT113-C07-2]] — Long euro: US banking issues stop the Fed from hiking massively while the ECB is hawkish.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C07-3|MT113-C07-3]] — Long yen: extremely undervalued and a BoJ yield-curve-control change eventually coming.

## [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311]]
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C03-3|MT114-C03-3]] — Mid-July to early August 2024: Trump's rising odds and pressure off Biden, growth fine so no Fed cut before the election, and BoJ only making noises; all three reversed within two weeks, producing the whipsaw.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C03-X1|MT114-C03-X1]] — The Bank of Japan had been reluctant to tighten because a stronger yen would risk torching the Japanese stock market, which was doing well on a weak yen and exports; this constraint is why markets treated BoJ talk as noise until it suddenly acted in August 2024.

## [[MT115 - New Challenges in the High Stakes World of Oil Trading - Oil & Money 2014]]
- [[MT115 - New Challenges in the High Stakes World of Oil Trading - Oil & Money 2014#^MT115-C03-3|MT115-C03-3]] — The $100-110 oil price was managed by Saudi/OPEC supply, helped by Iran sanctions and Libya, and not an equilibrium; prices should fall another $20-30.
- [[MT115 - New Challenges in the High Stakes World of Oil Trading - Oil & Money 2014#^MT115-C04-3|MT115-C04-3]] — OPEC alone cutting 2 mb/d each year would lose share to US shale and not stop price falls; stabilising needs slower US growth or new disruptions.
- [[MT115 - New Challenges in the High Stakes World of Oil Trading - Oil & Money 2014#^MT115-C08-2|MT115-C08-2]] — With low oil prices the oil-linked subsidy to gas disappears and gas prices must rise, so US gas could become more expensive than oil-indexed gas; LPG adjusts immediately.
- [[MT115 - New Challenges in the High Stakes World of Oil Trading - Oil & Money 2014#^MT115-C08-4|MT115-C08-4]] — China adjusts domestic fuel prices slowly, so demand response to lower oil is delayed.
- [[MT115 - New Challenges in the High Stakes World of Oil Trading - Oil & Money 2014#^MT115-C09-1|MT115-C09-1]] — India and Indonesia use low oil prices to deregulate fuel prices, so consumers face market prices and marginal demand from subsidised economies does not arrive.

## [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv]]
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C02-X1|MT117-C02-X1]] — Macro sentiment shifted substantially in the first months of 2024 from expected Fed cuts toward higher for longer, no cut or even a hike, which Baltas monitors as a driver of trend positioning.

## [[MT118 - Non-Trend Strategies, Trader Flows, and Risk Management at DCM - Open Interest 11]]
- [[MT118 - Non-Trend Strategies, Trader Flows, and Risk Management at DCM - Open Interest 11#^MT118-C10-1|MT118-C10-1]] — Macro models use inflation from CPI prints and from an in-house real-time nowcast built from equity sectors, giving holding periods of a month to a couple of months in fixed income and FX.

## [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341]]
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C03-1|MT119-C03-1]] — Recessions are typically caused by policy mistakes or participant errors rather than age of expansion, and current uncertainty is policy-made.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C04-X2|MT119-C04-X2]] — Policy uncertainty has stacked layers: erratic action (tariffs on, off, delayed), disputed economic impact of policies not used for a long time, and unknown central-bank reaction, so uncertainty is compounded.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C05-3|MT119-C05-3]] — Policy uncertainty causes firms to delay capex and consumers to delay big purchases, slowing real-economy decisions.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C09-1|MT119-C09-1]] — Whether a policy put exists matters: if stock prices keep falling, feedback loops into the real economy and monetary policy may eventually force a policy reversal, but the market was sceptical.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C09-2|MT119-C09-2]] — Uncertainty can be defined as ignorance of the policy reaction function (Taylor rule); with sticky 3% inflation, a December cut, September 50 bp cut and QT slowing from $25bn to $5bn, the Fed's function was unclear.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C09-X1|MT119-C09-X1]] — With inflation nearer 3% than the 2% target and household expectations above 3%, one could argue for raising rather than cutting rates, yet the Fed cut in September and December and slowed QT, so signals conflict and the reaction function is unclear.

## [[MT120 - October Market Recap- Missing Data, Strong Equities, and more - Systematic Investor - Ep.3]]
- [[MT120 - October Market Recap- Missing Data, Strong Equities, and more - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT120-C01-1|MT120-C01-1]] — Markets barely reacted to 40 days without official US economic data, questioning how much investors need routine data releases.
- [[MT120 - October Market Recap- Missing Data, Strong Equities, and more - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT120-C04-3|MT120-C04-3]] — Fixed income trends were hard because the market wants US cuts but they are not delivered, though risk in the asset class may now be lower.

## [[MT121 - Optimizing Investment Alpha with Customized Completion Portfolios Featuring Chris Jones, P]]
- [[MT121 - Optimizing Investment Alpha with Customized Completion Portfolios Featuring Chris Jones, P#^MT121-C14-1|MT121-C14-1]] — Periods of positive stock-bond correlation can be identified by hawkish Fed policy and an inflation shock.
- [[MT121 - Optimizing Investment Alpha with Customized Completion Portfolios Featuring Chris Jones, P#^MT121-C14-3|MT121-C14-3]] — Old academic papers find equities do very poorly in inflationary periods.
- [[MT121 - Optimizing Investment Alpha with Customized Completion Portfolios Featuring Chris Jones, P#^MT121-C15-1|MT121-C15-1]] — View that the then-current inflation is not permanent, so the bigger issue is low returns and loss of insurance from government bonds.

## [[MT122 - Paul Singer - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management]]
- [[MT122 - Paul Singer - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT122-C07-3|MT122-C07-3]] — Long-lived negative and zero interest rate policy encouraged excessive leverage in governments and markets.

## [[MT123 - Pierre Andurand Says the World Could Run Out of Cocoa Inventories - Odd Lots]]
- [[MT123 - Pierre Andurand Says the World Could Run Out of Cocoa Inventories - Odd Lots#^MT123-C09-4|MT123-C09-4]] — China's strategic reserve (SRB) is an extra inventory but would not be sold early in a multi-year deficit, so it cannot cap prices.
- [[MT123 - Pierre Andurand Says the World Could Run Out of Cocoa Inventories - Odd Lots#^MT123-C11-1|MT123-C11-1]] — The US pressures Ukraine not to hit Russian ports before the election to avoid high gasoline prices, but a more desperate Ukraine might attack them, creating a supply-disruption risk.
- [[MT123 - Pierre Andurand Says the World Could Run Out of Cocoa Inventories - Odd Lots#^MT123-C11-3|MT123-C11-3]] — A Trump presidency would probably enforce Iran sanctions more tightly, removing some Iranian oil, partly offset by more Saudi supply, so overall effect on oil prices is small.

## [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots]]
- [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots#^MT124-C04-X1|MT124-C04-X1]] — The shale supply response is self-limiting because producers know that rapid output growth would crash prices again and destroy profits, so shareholders push managers to hold output growth back even when prices are high.
- [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots#^MT124-C05-4|MT124-C05-4]] — Gulf producers (Saudi, Kuwait) and the US could add about 1.5 mb/d but only after Russian supply is visibly gone, not preemptively, against a potential 4 mb/d loss.
- [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots#^MT124-C06-1|MT124-C06-1]] — Sanctions would stay until the West trusts Russia again, which Andurand thinks requires regime change, so Russian oil would not return quickly even after a ceasefire.
- [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots#^MT124-C06-2|MT124-C06-2]] — Self-sanctioning by refiners, insurers and banks (no insurance for ships, no letters of credit even from Chinese banks) restricts Russian oil sales before formal sanctions.
- [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots#^MT124-C06-X1|MT124-C06-X1]] — The sanctions scenario is conditional on regime change: if a trusted government emerged quickly, Russian oil could return in a few months, but without it the supply stays out even after fighting stops, so the duration of the outage is the key swing factor for the oil price.
- [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots#^MT124-C07-1|MT124-C07-1]] — Strategic petroleum reserves of IEA members can release up to 5 mb/d for 12 months (about 2 mb/d for 2.5 years) but leave reserves empty and need refilling.
- [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots#^MT124-C07-X2|MT124-C07-X2]] — Demand destruction can come from government mandates rather than price: the Covid lockdown cut oil demand about 20%, whereas the shortfall now needs only about 2%, so mandates such as a few confinement days a month could close the gap without a recession.
- [[MT124 - Pierre Andurand on How We Might Get $200 a Barrel Oil - Odd Lots#^MT124-C08-7|MT124-C08-7]] — OPEC+ cut 10 mb/d in April 2020 and stayed compliant for over a year, restoring supply only gradually, which prevented a larger collapse.

## [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots]]
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C01-2|MT125-C01-2]] — Planning for zero Russian gas (exports to Europe already down 75%) makes the adjustment feasible and avoids the worst case of assuming supply that does not arrive.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C01-X2|MT125-C01-X2]] — The energy shock requires moving from a mindset of abundance to a war paradigm; once governments accept planning for no Russian gas, the demand-side steps needed are modest and manageable.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C02-X1|MT125-C02-X1]] — If Europe closes its gas balance through demand cuts and LNG, Russia loses about $100 billion of European gas revenue, so demand reduction is also a sanctions tool.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C03-5|MT125-C03-5]] — A G7 price cap on Russian oil is enforceable because most buyers gain from cheaper oil, though there will be some cheating.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C03-6|MT125-C03-6]] — To work the cap must be set below current market prices and below what China and India now pay, so that Russia still sells but earns less without cutting supply.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C04-X1|MT125-C04-X1]] — Very high power prices give operators a strong incentive to finish maintenance on the ageing French nuclear fleet, so part of the offline capacity should return, easing the power and gas squeeze.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C05-1|MT125-C05-1]] — Subsidies and consumer price caps (UK) leave demand above supply and risk shortages and blackouts; prices must reflect scarcity, and France's regulated prices only rose 4% while UK wholesale pass-through doubled.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C05-3|MT125-C05-3]] — Governments should run public awareness campaigns to cut demand, because price signals alone will not balance a market where supply is falling.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C06-1|MT125-C06-1]] — The US led the largest strategic reserve release ever (about 160 million barrels over six months, around 1 mb/d, plus 0.5 mb/d from others), which more than offset a Russian loss of only about 0.5 mb/d.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C06-4|MT125-C06-4]] — The EU embargo on Russian crude starts early December and on products early February, so actual Russian supply loss is still ahead.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C06-5|MT125-C06-5]] — Andurand expects to lose 1-2 mb/d of Russian supply, from the embargo, and from Putin's reaction to the price cap.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C06-6|MT125-C06-6]] — The SPR release is finite and slowing, so a supply source that depressed prices ends.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C06-7|MT125-C06-7]] — Chinese reopening could add about 2 mb/d of oil demand after lockdowns curbed consumption.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C09-5|MT125-C09-5]] — Energy security concerns accelerate decarbonization, because renewables and nuclear avoid funding autocratic suppliers; the problem is that decarbonization was not fast enough.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C10-1|MT125-C10-1]] — Coal and oil use must rise for two to three years to keep the lights on while low-carbon capacity is built as fast as possible.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C10-3|MT125-C10-3]] — Andurand became less bullish on EU carbon allowances because energy security will drive top-down mandates for nuclear and renewables, reducing the role of the carbon price.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C10-4|MT125-C10-4]] — Nationalization of utilities (such as Uniper) accelerates the shift to renewables and nuclear because governments act on energy security.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C11-5|MT125-C11-5]] — Sharp central-bank rate hikes plus high energy prices weaken consumer and business demand, creating a demand-side risk to oil prices.
- [[MT125 - Pierre Andurand on What Europe Needs to Do This Winter - Odd Lots#^MT125-C11-X2|MT125-C11-X2]] — Public awareness campaigns that cut non-essential demand do two things: avoid physical shortages and bring gas prices down significantly, which helps the global economy.

## [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338]]
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C01-3|MT126-C01-3]] — China is positioning as the free-trade advocate and pushing lower tariffs on trade partners while the US raises tariffs.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C02-X1|MT126-C02-X1]] — A US executive order establishing a federal crypto reserve was signed in early March 2025; it was not a surprise but marks a policy move toward state holding of crypto assets.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C03-X1|MT126-C03-X1]] — Jim argues the current administration is more deliberate than in 2016-20 (corporate elite involved, experienced Treasury leadership, time to plan), so its demand-cutting and 10-year-yield strategy should be read as a plan, not an accident.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C04-1|MT126-C04-1]] — The administration's stated top priority is lowering the 10-year yield, which it pursues by removing demand.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C04-X1|MT126-C04-X1]] — Supply-side economics works over the long term to support markets and can lower inflation at the same time, which is why the administration treats it as the only cheat code; the problems are political popularity and timing.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C04-X2|MT126-C04-X2]] — The administration may underestimate political limits: either it misreads history or assumes it can control the backlash from cutting demand among its own voters, which is a risk to the plan.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C05-3|MT126-C05-3]] — The administration wants markets and the economy to fall so the Fed is forced to stop QT and ease.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C05-4|MT126-C05-4]] — Treasury Secretary Bessent repeatedly cites a six to 12 month timeline, which Jim reads as the time to a QE-type response.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C05-X1|MT126-C05-X1]] — Washington DC real estate values fell about 20% in a month, a local sign of how fast federal job and spending cuts remove demand.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C05-X2|MT126-C05-X2]] — The demand cuts are deliberately front-loaded so any slowdown can be blamed on the previous administration, after which the new one takes credit for the recovery.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C05-X4|MT126-C05-X4]] — Jim forecasts quantitative tightening ending in March 2025 and a QE-like response within three to six months, in some form that may not be labelled QE.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C05-X5|MT126-C05-X5]] — Supply-side policies are the opposite of populism, so voter support is narrow (about 52/48 rather than 70/30), and with time that mismatch becomes a political problem for the administration.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C06-1|MT126-C06-1]] — US officials want a strong dollar, yet austerity combined with QE and lower rates usually weakens a currency; the contradiction is the strategy's weak point.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C06-2|MT126-C06-2]] — Markets can overwhelm official attempts to manage yields, the dollar and volatility.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C06-3|MT126-C06-3]] — Manipulating one price (yields) gets offset by market adjustment elsewhere (the dollar) unless the economy is a closed system like China.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C08-4|MT126-C08-4]] — A Mar-a-Lago-style accord forcing allies to buy 100-year zero-coupon US bonds is speculative; the US cannot bully the world indefinitely.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C09-X2|MT126-C09-X2]] — In a transactional regime that ignores orthodoxy, previously unthinkable policy shifts (like ending the gold link in 1971) can happen, so forecasts should not rule out unconventional debt solutions for lack of precedent.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C12-2|MT126-C12-2]] — A softening of US-China rhetoric after the election was one reason Jim turned bullish on Chinese equities.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C14-3|MT126-C14-3]] — Erdogan's preference for loose monetary policy conflicts with 40% rates, so keeping rates high as conditions worsen is doubtful.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C15-1|MT126-C15-1]] — A Turkish currency crisis is a decent chance given the weak economy, upcoming elections and Russia pressure.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C15-X1|MT126-C15-X1]] — As US support for the alliance weakens, Turkey (second-largest NATO army, about 100 million people, Russia counterweight) becomes more strategic for Europe, which may bring European support and so partly offsets the currency-crisis risk.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C17-3|MT126-C17-3]] — A large market fall leads to political backlash (job losses, wealthy donors) and policy reversal, as with Nixon's price controls.
- [[MT126 - Politics, Markets & Power Plays - Systematic Investor 338#^MT126-C17-4|MT126-C17-4]] — Markets are a 'fifth state' that check government policy; a market lead on political outcomes.

## [[MT127 - Portable Alpha, Trend Following & AI Investing ft. Harry Moore - Systematic Investor - Ep]]
- [[MT127 - Portable Alpha, Trend Following & AI Investing ft. Harry Moore - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT127-C02-3|MT127-C02-3]] — Joint central-bank intervention in the yen hit trend followers who were short yen, though past interventions did not last long.
- [[MT127 - Portable Alpha, Trend Following & AI Investing ft. Harry Moore - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT127-C02-X1|MT127-C02-X1]] — The yen kept weakening even after the Bank of Japan began raising rates following 30 years of near-zero rates, so the rate-differential story failed to hold the currency; authorities then intervened, as they had a couple of times in the last two to three years without lasting effect.
- [[MT127 - Portable Alpha, Trend Following & AI Investing ft. Harry Moore - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT127-C03-4|MT127-C03-4]] — Authorities intervene when a move has run so far for so long that natural market forces are not reversing it.
- [[MT127 - Portable Alpha, Trend Following & AI Investing ft. Harry Moore - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT127-C08-1|MT127-C08-1]] — With inflation above target for many years, the Fed may not be able to cut rates to rescue markets, leaving bonds also vulnerable to a hard sell-off.

## [[MT128 - Position Sizing When Markets Break feat. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.386]]
- [[MT128 - Position Sizing When Markets Break feat. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.386#^MT128-C07-X1|MT128-C07-X1]] — Kevin Warsh was named as the nominee for Fed chair around the time of the 30 January 2026 silver crash, a surprise relative to expectations, and was taken by many as the proximate cause of the precious-metals sell-off.
- [[MT128 - Position Sizing When Markets Break feat. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.386#^MT128-C08-2|MT128-C08-2]] — The bond market reaction to the new Fed chair nomination was muted (no 50bp two-year move), so it was probably not the genuine cause of the silver crash.
- [[MT128 - Position Sizing When Markets Break feat. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.386#^MT128-C08-3|MT128-C08-3]] — Whether the new Fed chair will cut rates for the president or act independently is unknowable, though he is seen as relatively sane and possibly hawkish.
- [[MT128 - Position Sizing When Markets Break feat. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.386#^MT128-C08-X1|MT128-C08-X1]] — The Fed chair nominee holds less mainstream views on QE and the Fed balance sheet but is seen as conventional or even hawkish on interest-rate setting, so the policy mix could differ from what the president wants.
- [[MT128 - Position Sizing When Markets Break feat. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.386#^MT128-C09-X1|MT128-C09-X1]] — A president's appointment says little about a Fed chair's later independence: Powell was appointed by Trump and then resisted pressure, and once in office chairs cannot be removed and care about their legacy.
- [[MT128 - Position Sizing When Markets Break feat. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.386#^MT128-C09-X2|MT128-C09-X2]] — The Fed chair is one of 12 FOMC votes and cannot set rates alone; the real independence risk is a pending Supreme Court ruling that may let the president remove governors and pack the board.

## [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321]]
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C04-X3|MT129-C04-X3]] — An RFK Jr health role is flagged as a policy risk for the pharmaceutical sector because he is no fan of big pharma; posed as an open question, not a conclusion.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C05-1|MT129-C05-1]] — Tariffs are more a bargaining chip than an end game, though they cannot be ruled out as permanent.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C05-2|MT129-C05-2]] — Bessent's shadow Fed chair idea could undermine the Fed's independence.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C05-X3|MT129-C05-X3]] — Musk's net worth rose by about $26.5bn (Bloomberg) on the day after the election, an immediate repricing of the person and firms seen as winners from a Trump victory despite the large sums he spent.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C06-5|MT129-C06-5]] — China's policy measures aimed at stabilising asset markets rather than sustaining a growth rebound.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C07-2|MT129-C07-2]] — Tariffs raise price levels once rather than the inflation rate, so the Fed may look through them if expectations stay anchored.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C07-3|MT129-C07-3]] — Tariff bluster may lead to a US-China deal as in 2019-2020.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C07-4|MT129-C07-4]] — Markets are pricing optimism on supply-side policy (tax cuts, deregulation) that raises growth without inflation.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C07-X1|MT129-C07-X1]] — The Trump tax cuts are expected to be extended and a cut of the corporate rate to 15% is possible but uncertain; these supply-side measures are part of what equities are pricing.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C08-1|MT129-C08-1]] — Republicans control both houses and can use reconciliation for budget measures for two years, though moderates may limit spending and tax cuts.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C08-4|MT129-C08-4]] — Bessent as Treasury pick may moderate Trump's weaker-dollar impulse and favour deficit reduction towards 3%.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C08-X1|MT129-C08-X1]] — The rise in Treasury yields after the election reflects optimism about growth from lower taxes and deregulation as well as deficit worries, so the move is a growth plus supply mix and not purely a fiscal scare.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C09-1|MT129-C09-1]] — Long yields rose from the day of the Fed's 50bp cut, counter to the aim of lowering yields across the curve.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C09-2|MT129-C09-2]] — JP Morgan revised its Fed path: cuts every other meeting, ending around 3.5%.
- [[MT129 - Post-Election Trends - Systematic Investor 321#^MT129-C10-X3|MT129-C10-X3]] — Removal of the election uncertainty could restore trend traction in late 2024, since trends can resume once the policy direction is clearer.

## [[MT130 - QIS & Trend Following in 2026 ft. Nick Baltas - Systematic Investor - Ep.401]]
- [[MT130 - QIS & Trend Following in 2026 ft. Nick Baltas - Systematic Investor - Ep.401#^MT130-C03-1|MT130-C03-1]] — 2026 theme shifted from AI capex boosting profits to a geopolitical shock in March (Middle East) and a pivot from pricing rate cuts to debating hikes, reviving 2022 memories.

## [[MT131 - Redefining Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.363]]
