- [[MT170 - The Market Creates Its Own Trends — Richard Brennan - Systematic Investor - Ep.418#^MT170-C04-1|MT170-C04-1]] — Mid-September 2026: Fed hike 95% priced, US 10-year at 5%, Bund 3.55%, Brent 108 (physical cargoes above $130); an energy and rates tightening backdrop that Rich considers beneficial for trend following.

## [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397]]
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C02-1|MT171-C02-1]] — Having hedge fund managers at Treasury and the Fed signals policy has shifted from plumbing and money supply to actively managing market outcomes via supply and demand in markets.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C02-X1|MT171-C02-X1]] — Central-bank independence from political reach has broken down dramatically (seen in the Warsh confirmation hearing), so Fed policy should be modelled as political and outcome-driven rather than data-driven.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C05-2|MT171-C05-2]] — Military build-up accelerated after the Iran ceasefire (60-70% more troops and flights), so the ceasefire should not be read as de-escalation for markets.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C08-3|MT171-C08-3]] — The WTI-Brent spread should widen again because the US will control domestic oil prices while geopolitical supply disruption raises non-US benchmarks.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C08-X2|MT171-C08-X2]] — Authorities can manage equity-index levels but not oil and other commodities (especially non-US benchmarks), so worsening geopolitics should show up in oil and spreads while indices are propped; assuming a one-to-one index reaction to geopolitics ignores the dominant actor offsetting it.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C12-1|MT171-C12-1]] — Authorities will keep managing markets so conditions deteriorate while the indices muddle through; the cost is erosion of the dollar's exorbitant privilege and moral/ally capital.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C13-1|MT171-C13-1]] — The gap between market levels and real-world risk is a feature because the administration's incentive is to create liquidity and support while risk builds, so indices can levitate for years even as oil dispersion and conditions worsen.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C14-X1|MT171-C14-X1]] — Paulson's call to prepare for a Treasury demand shortfall works as a pre-emptive 'whatever it takes' backstop: a visible bazooka deters the stress event, and the clear path of what authorities will do is part of why markets stay up; the difference from 2008 is acting before, not after, the crisis.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C15-1|MT171-C15-1]] — Controlling the 10-year yield controls equity valuations, so authorities will push yields lower to support markets.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C15-X1|MT171-C15-X1]] — Historical analogy: a politically aligned new Fed chair cutting rates for political reasons repeats the Arthur Burns episode of the 1970s, which let inflation re-accelerate.
- [[MT171 - The Market Isn’t Free Anymore — It’s Being Managed - Systematic Investor - Ep.397#^MT171-C16-X1|MT171-C16-X1]] — The incoming Fed chair uses AI-driven disinflation as the central argument for easier policy, so the AI-deflation narrative has a policy purpose; Jim notes nobody can say how fast any such deflation would arrive.

## [[MT172 - The Market's Biggest Warning Signs Right Now with Todd Sohn - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep]]
- [[MT172 - The Market's Biggest Warning Signs Right Now with Todd Sohn - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep#^MT172-C12-X1|MT172-C12-X1]] — The two-year Treasury yield has risen recently, raising the possibility that the Fed has to tighten; Sohn offers this tentatively and defers to colleagues on rates.

## [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313]]
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C01-1|MT176-C01-1]] — Jackson Hole's theme each year shows what policymakers see as the dominant macro problem (2022 supply constraints, 2023 debt, 2024 transmission of rate hikes), a read on the policy regime.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C03-2|MT176-C03-2]] — Central banks will increasingly accept higher inflation rather than higher unemployment when a supply shock hits, because political support for fighting inflation is thinner than for protecting jobs.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C06-3|MT176-C06-3]] — Powell's Jackson Hole speech signalled the start of an easing cycle, not just a September cut, so the destination of policy matters more than the 25 versus 50bp debate.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C06-4|MT176-C06-4]] — Historically the Fed opens easing cycles with 25bp and moves to inter-meeting or larger cuts only when conditions deteriorate (2001, 2007), the typical playbook.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C07-1|MT176-C07-1]] — The Fed's long-run neutral rate estimate (long-term dot) was 2.5% for years and has been edged up to about 2.8% in recent projections, with arguments for 3% on productivity and fiscal expansion.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C07-2|MT176-C07-2]] — Neutral rate is unobservable in real time (range roughly 2.5-3.5%), so with policy at 5-5.25% there is still much easing room, which the market prices as about 100bp by year end.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C07-3|MT176-C07-3]] — Market pricing of rates near 3% by mid-next year equals a move to neutral plus some probability of going below neutral on recession risk (one forecaster calls for 2%).
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C07-4|MT176-C07-4]] — A data-dependent Fed reacts quickly to weak data: a very negative payrolls print would force a 50bp cut at once, so rate moves become more reactive to individual releases.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C07-X1|MT176-C07-X1]] — The Fed's long-run funds rate (the r-star proxy) is built as a 0.5% real neutral rate plus 2% inflation, so its drift from 2.5% to 2.8% reflects a higher real-neutral assumption rather than inflation.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C07-X2|MT176-C07-X2]] — Dunne's call for the first cut was 25bp rather than 50bp, despite 75bp hikes on the way up and the argument for reaching neutral faster, because the Fed historically starts cycles at 25bp.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C08-1|MT176-C08-1]] — The shift from forecast-based to data-dependent policy means higher rates volatility around data releases and around political risk such as the US election.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C08-2|MT176-C08-2]] — Forward guidance, introduced after the financial crisis, coincides with the extreme carry regime; central banks are accused of facilitating it by suppressing volatility.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C08-3|MT176-C08-3]] — Policy stance cannot be summarised by one short rate: long yields, equities and credit spreads determine overall financial conditions, so policy can still be loose when the policy rate is called restrictive.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C08-X2|MT176-C08-X2]] — Forward guidance was added after the financial crisis to anchor long-term rates by promising low short rates, and has since evolved into central banks announcing the intended path, the step Kaastrup-Larsen links to suppressed volatility.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C08-X3|MT176-C08-X3]] — Kaastrup-Larsen wants the Fed to cut only 25bp and approves that it did not react to the August volatility spike, while ex-NY Fed president Dudley argued for 50bp; he sees reacting to a volatility event as feeding the carry/Fed-put regime.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C09-X1|MT176-C09-X1]] — Adam Posen holds that financial conditions matter more than the policy rate and criticises excessive central bank communication; Dunne's conclusion is that the current approach means more rates volatility and market jitters.
- [[MT176 - The Paradox of High-Volatility Alternatives - Systematic Investor 313#^MT176-C12-X1|MT176-C12-X1]] — Japanese monetary policy is the root cause of the yen carry trade, which the BIS paper omits; he concludes carry trades will always exist, so unwinds recur.

## [[MT178 - The Strategy That Thrives When Markets Panic - Systematic Investor - Ep.403]]
- [[MT178 - The Strategy That Thrives When Markets Panic - Systematic Investor - Ep.403#^MT178-C06-1|MT178-C06-1]] — In May 2026 short-term managers beat trend and diversifying managers; all groups benefit mainly from equities and commodities while fixed income is challenging because the Fed moved from expected cuts to possible hikes.

## [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398]]
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C02-1|MT182-C02-1]] — Market behaviour depends on whether the new Fed chair follows political pressure to cut rates or his earlier stance that the Fed balance sheet should shrink; a balance-sheet-shrinking chair would matter a lot for markets.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C04-1|MT182-C04-1]] — CTA fixed-income positioning turned short in March 2026 on expected inflationary pressure from the Middle East conflict, in a sector that had been difficult with long/short exposure flipping every 6-8 weeks.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C06-3|MT182-C06-3]] — Supply shocks (such as the oil shock from the Middle East conflict) are harder for central banks than demand shocks, since cutting rates to boost demand worsens inflation; this leaves policy unsure and prices free to trend.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C06-X2|MT182-C06-X2]] — Euro-area inflation is about 3% and US inflation stays persistently above 2% (trimmed-mean view), which leaves central banks without a clear response to the supply shock and supports the 1970s-style regime argument.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C09-2|MT182-C09-2]] — Government intervention is the main risk to trends; in a supply shock the proper policy response is unclear, so prices are driven by market behaviour rather than policy.
- [[MT182 - The VUCA World Is Here - Systematic Investor - Ep.398#^MT182-C09-3|MT182-C09-3]] — Fed intervention in March 2020 caused reversals but was followed by one of the strongest periods for trend following because the stimulus proved very inflationary.

## [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303]]
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C12-1|MT183-C12-1]] — Policy outcomes are counterintuitive: just as Nixon opened China, Trump may make a deal with China; the initial protectionist or inflationary tariff trade may be right at first but should not be overextended.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C12-X1|MT183-C12-X1]] — Because the market still doubts that Biden remains the Democratic candidate, it is not too late to position for a Trump win (steepener, gold, vol); this is like buying out-of-the-money puts, with the risk of buy-the-rumour-sell-the-news into the election.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C12-X2|MT183-C12-X2]] — Replacing Biden is structurally hard: his roughly $100 million of campaign funds can legally pass only to Vice President Harris, who is unpopular, and party-base constraints rule out most alternatives, so the window is narrow and the same Trump-favoured outcome likely stays.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C13-3|MT183-C13-3]] — Playing a Trump win: expect a yield steepener, gold higher and volatility up, with the 10-year back to 5% by year end.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C13-X2|MT183-C13-X2]] — After the debate the Trump trade lifted yields for a few days, but payrolls beat while unemployment ticked up, so bonds had not moved decisively and a Trump win was not obviously priced in.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C14-1|MT183-C14-1]] — A Trump administration would run the economy hot with a Fed chair change (like Nixon and Burns), pricing higher inflation and a steepener, a trade not heavily positioned.
- [[MT183 - The Volatility Apocalypse - Systematic Investor 303#^MT183-C15-X2|MT183-C15-X2]] — European banks hold sovereign debt, so political stress hits bank equities twice (directly and via sovereign bond losses on their balance sheets); the ECB has tools to intervene but signals it will let markets play out.

## [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)]]
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C02-2|MT184-C02-2]] — Russia is unusually well placed to resist sanctions as a top-three gas, oil and coal producer, yet sanctions and export controls are starting to bite: GDP fell perhaps about 5% rather than 10%, and over 1,200 Western companies left or scaled back.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C02-X2|MT184-C02-X2]] — Sanctions and export controls are continually tightened to close circumvention routes through third countries, which is why Petraeus expects their economic bite on Russia to grow over time.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C03-1|MT184-C03-1]] — A frozen conflict would leave Russia under sanctions and Ukraine under bombardment, so negotiations become possible only when Putin recognizes the war is unsustainable, with rebuilding and security guarantees needed afterwards.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C06-1|MT184-C06-1]] — The West's earlier caution to avoid provoking Putin failed; Ukraine neutrality or Crimea lease ideas floated before the invasion may be part of an eventual settlement, with a Marshall Plan and security guarantee; Finland and Sweden will join NATO.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C06-X1|MT184-C06-X1]] — Russian domestic pressure, including a possible challenge to Putin, is one factor that could force the realisation that the war is unsustainable, which is the precondition for negotiations.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C06-X2|MT184-C06-X2]] — Pre-war ideas of neutrality or a Crimea lease may be hard to revive because about 95% of Ukrainians want all of Ukraine liberated, so Ukrainian domestic politics constrains any compromise.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C09-1|MT184-C09-1]] — Deterrence rests on credible capabilities and clear will to use them; doubt about US will after Afghanistan and the Syria red line weakens it, while the US response on Ukraine restored credibility. Competition with China must be managed so it does not become conflict.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C10-1|MT184-C10-1]] — China reversed zero-COVID, which Petraeus reads as a sign it can learn and might also reverse wolf-warrior diplomacy, economic coercion (Australia) and border clashes; this would allow a more constructive relationship despite severe competition.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C10-X1|MT184-C10-X1]] — Reversing zero-COVID without mRNA vaccines and with weak elderly vaccination predictably produces a surge in Chinese COVID deaths that may ultimately exceed the US toll of over a million.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C10-X2|MT184-C10-X2]] — Wolf-warrior diplomacy carried economic costs: it led the EU to shelve its investment agreement with China and prompted other countries to respond, so reversing it could restore investment ties.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C14-2|MT184-C14-2]] — US export controls on advanced chips, fabrication equipment and IP will likely be more restrictions with higher fences around fewer items, affecting investment limits such as entity lists, dual-use technology and data flows.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C14-X1|MT184-C14-X1]] — Real-time sharing of cyber threat information is the missing big idea because US Congress has been reluctant to compel prompt reporting (fiduciary and stock-price concerns), so alerts take hours or days rather than machine speed.
- [[MT184 - The War in Ukraine, Relations With China & Wider Geopolitics (With General David Petraeus)#^MT184-C14-X2|MT184-C14-X2]] — Although the most advanced chips are made in Taiwan and Korea with Dutch and German machines, everything rests on US intellectual property, so US export controls bind the whole semiconductor supply chain and are likely to widen.

## [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382]]
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C02-2|MT186-C02-2]] — In the globalized era coordinated central bank action (QE) absorbed imbalances quickly and truncated trends, which made conditions harder for trend followers.

## [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414]]
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C01-3|MT187-C01-3]] — Joint Japan MoF and Fed yen intervention signals authorities dislike the market direction, but FX intervention usually fails to reverse trends.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C06-2|MT187-C06-2]] — The Treasury buyback programme (raised from about $2bn to about $4bn a month) swaps off-the-run for new on-the-run issuance, changing composition not total debt, which signals insufficient dealer market-making capacity.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C06-X1|MT187-C06-X1]] — Treasury buybacks have precedent: the first programme (2000-2002) was designed when budget surpluses raised fears of running short of long bonds, was stopped when rates were stable and restarted later, so the current expansion reflects liquidity needs rather than a surplus.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C07-1|MT187-C07-1]] — Buyback announced right after the long bond hit 5.34% looks like soft yield curve control for the long end.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C08-1|MT187-C08-1]] — Treasury buybacks can act as a small bond market stabiliser by buying off-the-run bonds that trade cheap to the curve.

## [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep]]
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C02-1|MT189-C02-1]] — Rising yields are gradually ticking higher and are not getting enough attention.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C04-2|MT189-C04-2]] — Yen intervention caused little damage to CTAs despite crowded short-yen exposure; Beer's 10-market portfolio even outperformed in those days.

## [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422]]
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C01-1|MT191-C01-1]] — Managed futures benefit in a chaotic geopolitical and inflationary environment, so 2026 was seen as an ideal year for them.

## [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3]]
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C02-X1|MT192-C02-X1]] — Multilateral bodies such as the G7 are losing their ability to move the world, as shown by how little the June 2025 summit registered, in a world dominated by a few individuals, narratives and social media; so policy-meeting headlines carry less market-moving weight.

## [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297]]
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C01-2|MT193-C01-2]] — A report says Trump advisors plan to make the president more influential over Fed rate decisions, which Trump previously signalled with calls for lower rates and a weaker dollar.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C02-1|MT193-C02-1]] — Interfering with Fed independence would be dollar negative and push bond yields higher.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C03-X1|MT193-C03-X1]] — Polls before the June 2024 European elections show a swing to the right in several large countries (Germany, France, possibly Spain), which could change the European Parliament's composition; Niels asks whether investors should treat it as a risk.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C04-3|MT193-C04-3]] — One reason the US economy resisted higher rates is that borrowers locked in low rates through refinancing.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C05-1|MT193-C05-1]] — Long and variable monetary policy lags mean the impact of past rate hikes may still arrive.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C05-X1|MT193-C05-X1]] — The Fed's error was pre-announcing rate cuts for 2024 in December 2023 without the data to support them, which made it look as if it was flip-flopping once the data turned and explains the accusation of excess data dependence.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C06-1|MT193-C06-1]] — Fed Governor Waller's reaction function: hikes are very unlikely and the next move is likely a cut, requiring several months of good inflation or a significant labor market weakening.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C06-2|MT193-C06-2]] — Central banks moved from forward guidance to month-by-month data dependence because their models failed to forecast the inflation surge.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C06-X1|MT193-C06-X1]] — Until about 2020 central banks set policy from a two-year-ahead inflation forecast (fan charts) and deliberately ignored the latest print because it reflects past policy; after the 2021-22 inflation miss they abandoned this approach.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C06-X2|MT193-C06-X2]] — Month-by-month data-dependent central banking replaces forward guidance and so creates uncertainty and ambiguity about the rate path, defeating the stabilising purpose of guidance.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C07-1|MT193-C07-1]] — Central banks react to tiny differences in monthly inflation prints, which are sampled estimates with seasonal adjustment errors, making them hypersensitive and rudderless.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C07-X1|MT193-C07-X1]] — Bernanke's review of Bank of England forecasting recommends moving from point estimates and fan charts to scenario analysis; Waller doubts it helps because scenarios simply say higher inflation means higher rates and lower inflation lower rates, leaving central banks 'rudderless'.

## [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344]]
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C01-1|MT194-C01-1]] — Fed Chair Powell said tariffs were much bigger than expected, creating tension in the dual mandate and effectively putting the Fed on hold.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C02-1|MT194-C02-1]] — Removing Powell from the Fed would worsen the turmoil.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C03-4|MT194-C03-4]] — Short-term interest rate positions, along with gold and energies, were bright spots in April 2025, though not universal across countries (Europe strongest).
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C04-1|MT194-C04-1]] — Bonds are hard to trade: foreign selling of US assets plus tariff inflation could cause a huge curve steepener even if short-end rates are cut.

## [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326]]
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C07-1|MT195-C07-1]] — The US-vs-world dollar trade had legs in Dec 2024 but faced risks of policy mistakes, geopolitical conflict and trade conflict, creating turbulence trend likes.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C12-1|MT195-C12-1]] — In the last 2 to 3 years volatility and correlation profiles have focused on monetary policy and inflation, driving the stock-bond correlation.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C16-3|MT195-C16-3]] — The US lagging other regions in cutting rates, a divergence not seen in most careers, makes the pro-US theme worth watching.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C16-4|MT195-C16-4]] — Tariffs plus animal spirits could force the Fed to tighten while the ECB eases, an unprecedented divergence in recent decades.

## [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346]]
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C02-4|MT196-C02-4]] — If policy produces stagflation (stagnation plus tariff inflation), the normal central bank reaction of cutting rates when growth weakens may not apply, so bonds hedge less.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C13-3|MT196-C13-3]] — Rob, already underweight US stocks, sold remaining US stock exposure before Liberation Day and went to cash in his long-only portfolio, his biggest discretionary decision ever, outside his systematic portfolio.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C14-1|MT196-C14-1]] — Rob expects a deep technical US recession as tariffs hit goods already in transit; Q1 GDP was unaffected by tariffs, and CEOs are reluctant to give honest forward earnings, so the real-economy effect and earnings downgrades come later and the market should not stay where it is.

## [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314]]
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C03-5|MT197-C03-5]] — Central bank policies over decades fostered the carry regime (forward guidance reducing uncertainty).
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C04-1|MT197-C04-1]] — The Fed's 50bp cut in Sept 2024 came with maximum market uncertainty (50/50 pricing the day before, versus usual 90-95% certainty), contradicting its forward guidance aim; a 50bp move usually signals a major policy reversal.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C04-2|MT197-C04-2]] — After the 50bp cut the dollar did not set a new low and 10- and 30-year Treasury prices fell, the opposite of the usual response, suggesting long-end investors did not want to hold bonds.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C04-3|MT197-C04-3]] — Atlanta Fed GDPNow was around 3% for the next quarter, well above Blue Chip consensus, so the Fed's 50bp cut looked odd given strong growth, above-target inflation and no recession precipice.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C05-X2|MT197-C05-X2]] — The September 2024 Fed cut was foreshadowed at the August Jackson Hole speeches (Powell: price stability under control, focus turning to the employment leg of the dual mandate), so the policy pivot itself was telegraphed even though the size (25 vs 50bp) was not.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C06-2|MT197-C06-2]] — A 50bp cut with equities at all-time highs is nearly unprecedented, and with low volatility and low uncertainty the cut looks rash; markets were not signalling a problem.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C06-X1|MT197-C06-X1]] — Reading David Dredge: central banks never attribute the later inflation to their own extreme policies (zero rates, QE aimed at generating inflation), credit their latest tightening for restoring price stability, and blame exogenous shocks (Bailey cited Covid/Ukraine about 50 times), so their narratives are self-serving and a weak guide to future policy.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C15-X1|MT197-C15-X1]] — A 20% equity bear market would only take back about a year and a half of returns and not remove the overvaluation, yet policymakers (calls for a 75bp cut after the August 2024 carry unwind) act to prevent it; this shows over-sensitivity of policy to modest equity declines.

## [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342]]
- [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342#^MT198-C01-1|MT198-C01-1]] — Q1 2025 was a quarter of building uncertainty culminating in equities being pummelled around the universal tariff announcement.

## [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316]]
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C02-1|MT200-C02-1]] — Large wage settlements (dock workers 62% over 6 years, Boeing rejecting 30%) signal a shift in labour bargaining power that could spark inflation; EU 45% EV tariffs and higher energy prices add to price pressure.
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C02-2|MT200-C02-2]] — China's stimulus in Sept 2024 boosted Chinese equities and propagated into commodities such as iron ore after a year of falling prices.

## [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8]]
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C11-2|MT201-C11-2]] — The 2009-2020 lost decade (zero rates, QE) had few outliers, was artificial, and long quiet periods are followed by bigger-than-average moves, as seen now.

## [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307]]
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C03-3|MT202-C03-3]] — Government bonds rose 1.78% in July 2024 as growth and rate narratives shifted, while equities were up about 1.7% (MSCI World) and S&P 500 about 1.25%.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C07-2|MT202-C07-2]] — Investors accept carry-trade tails because they expect policy backstops, which is part of why carry makes money over the long run.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C08-1|MT202-C08-1]] — If Trump wins in 2024 he would likely also take the Senate and keep the House, giving a broad mandate; the Supreme Court immunity ruling adds executive scope.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C08-2|MT202-C08-2]] — Tariffs/protectionism, immigration restriction and an activist Fed are each inflationary and together bullish for hard assets; a hot economy favoured over global growth.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C09-X1|MT202-C09-X1]] — Jim expects a second Trump term to follow the consolidation playbook he saw in Turkey (judiciary first, then military, then other checks and balances), with the Supreme Court already remade, while the Biden administration can do little before the election because its actions would be overturned.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C11-3|MT202-C11-3]] — The Fed is reflexive to markets because market collateral moves money supply, which is why Powell signalled action if markets did something weird; expect Fed to react to a market fall.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C11-X1|MT202-C11-X1]] — After the July 2024 payrolls (unemployment 4.3%), markets priced about 100 bp of Fed cuts by year end, a number that had swung around several times already that year, which shows how fast rate expectations reprice on labour data.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C12-2|MT202-C12-2]] — The Fed can reverse from cutting to tightening and back quickly (late 2018) depending on market action, so expect a policy turn if markets stay down into Jackson Hole.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C12-3|MT202-C12-3]] — Rate hikes had slow effects because households locked in rates and demand was sequestered; lowering rates now releases accumulated demand, so cuts may have a larger effect than hikes.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C13-3|MT202-C13-3]] — Japanese authorities intervene in equity markets directly because they recognise the wealth effect; a US analogue is possible.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C17-2|MT202-C17-2]] — The two biggest drivers of path change are the Fed reaction function (50 bp cut or strong language at Jackson Hole) and election policy.

## [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent]]
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C03-3|MT203-C03-3]] — The 1992 ERM trade: the Bank of England was obliged to defend the 2.5% lower band by buying unlimited sterling, so the speculator could push until the peg broke.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C04-1|MT203-C04-1]] — Floating-rate UK mortgages meant rate hikes to defend the pound crushed the housing market and thus the economy, so the defence was self-defeating.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C07-X1|MT203-C07-X1]] — Bessent expects the US-China decoupling to continue under either party, with sanctions held back until after the 5 November 2024 election and the process likely to accelerate under Trump.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C09-3|MT203-C09-3]] — Treasury bill-heavy refunding and Powell's dovish press conferences in Nov-Dec 2023 loosened financial conditions, lifting stocks 25% and tightening high yield spreads, reigniting the economy.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C09-4|MT203-C09-4]] — The Fed was late to hike and was doing QE until the month before the first hike in 2022, allowing inflation to run.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C10-1|MT203-C10-1]] — The Abenomics trade was simple: short the yen and long Japanese equities, with no JGB exposure because JGB behaviour was uncertain.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C10-3|MT203-C10-3]] — Despite correct Japanese reflation analysis, Key Square expected a stronger yen from policy normalisation and lost because the yen weakened further, a lesson that valuation does not drive currencies near term.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C10-5|MT203-C10-5]] — Prime Minister Kishida's popularity was tied to the yen; a rational political response would be to act, but weakness persisted, and he expected him to be out in about two months.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C11-1|MT203-C11-1]] — With large personal and corporate savings, rate hikes stimulate Japan's economy, so the BoJ need not fall prey to fiscal dominance (government unable to raise rates because of debt).
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C14-1|MT203-C14-1]] — The next election cycle could feature populists on both sides, with policy such as banning stock buybacks, a risk for equity valuation.

## [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343]]
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C02-1|MT205-C02-1]] — In the April 2025 tariff shock the 10-year Treasury yield had its largest 3-day jump since 2001, failing as a safe haven.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C02-3|MT205-C02-3]] — In the tariff shock Treasuries and the dollar fell together while Japanese yields dropped, a unique cross-country divergence; the 30-year touched 5%.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C03-1|MT205-C03-1]] — The Treasury rout probably forced the tariff pause because a bond market collapse is intolerable, unlike a temporary equity or goods price fall.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C05-1|MT205-C05-1]] — Tariff shock caused wide country dispersion in bonds and FX (Australia/NZ yields up, Japan down; Brazil and Mexico currencies fell despite low tariffs), so tariff size did not map to price moves.

## [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315]]
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C06-X1|MT206-C06-X1]] — Chinese policymakers timed a 'whatever it takes' stimulus to coincide with positive global year-end flows, which helps Chinese assets and adds only a modest cushion against downside for developed markets rather than a major spillover.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C06-X2|MT206-C06-X2]] — Commentators doubted the Chinese stimulus would change consumer spending or the economy's trajectory; the measures were liquidity injections aimed at the equity market, so the rebound is market support rather than fundamentals.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C08-1|MT206-C08-1]] — Core PCE year-on-year was stuck near 2.7% even as the Fed message shifted to labor market downside risk.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C08-2|MT206-C08-2]] — When cyclical inflation falls and the Fed eases, the long end of the curve sells off (a bear steepener) because investors price structural inflation returning.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C10-1|MT206-C10-1]] — Interest rate effects are non-linear: high rates hold back demand (e.g. home purchases) that is released inelastically once rates fall, so rate cuts re-accelerate the economy.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C10-2|MT206-C10-2]] — After the Fed's December 2023 pivot, the feared bad news did not arrive; pent-up demand returned as the 10-year fell and the economy re-accelerated.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C10-5|MT206-C10-5]] — Every Fed cut should steepen the curve because it feeds longer-term inflation expectations while lowering the front end, so Cham stays in curve steepeners.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C10-X1|MT206-C10-X1]] — Rate cuts release pent-up demand only once; repeated cuts become less effective as the sequestered demand is used up.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C10-X2|MT206-C10-X2]] — When the long end starts to reject 'transitory', rising long-term yields feed back into inflation, reinforcing the structural regime.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C11-4|MT206-C11-4]] — Governments have an incentive to make the short term look good in election years, which supports markets.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C12-2|MT206-C12-2]] — A Trump win would be stimulative with pressure on the Fed (Nixon/Erdogan analogy): a big short-term market positive but worse structural inflation.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C13-1|MT206-C13-1]] — A Harris win means redistribution via capital gains tax and is more measured, but still inflationary and possibly worse for markets near term.
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C15-X1|MT206-C15-X1]] — The Supreme Court ruling increases presidential power, which magnifies policy risk, especially under unified government, so Cham expects bigger policy moves than usual.

## [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro]]
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C04-3|MT208-C04-3]] — A weaker dollar adds inflation pressure and forces the Fed to tighten more, a feedback loop.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C05-2|MT208-C05-2]] — An aggressive Fed move (shrinking the $9T balance sheet fast) would lift rates and the dollar and hurt markets.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C05-X1|MT208-C05-X1]] — Gromen admits the dollar's drop may be only a small counter-cyclical move and that it is too early to tell; the outcome depends on how aggressive the Fed is (a fast balance-sheet cut would push rates and the dollar up and markets down).
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C06-1|MT208-C06-1]] — A flattening yield curve signals that the bond market is near its tolerance for tightening.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C06-2|MT208-C06-2]] — The Fed can only deliver the expected four hikes and balance sheet runoff if the dollar falls sharply (DXY toward 70-80).
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C06-3|MT208-C06-3]] — The dollar is zero sum: other central banks may ease to stop their currencies strengthening so the dollar falls only so far.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C06-X1|MT208-C06-X1]] — On 12 January 2022 DXY was about 94 (high 97, about 89 a year earlier, range-bound until May-June 2021, then up after the Fed turned hawkish), and it had not been below 80 since 2014, so Gromen's 70-80 scenario means an extreme move.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C07-3|MT208-C07-3]] — Host extrapolates yield trend lines across maturities to a curve inversion around July 2022 with 30-year yield at most about 2.7%.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C08-2|MT208-C08-2]] — Working-class wages rose faster than inflation for the first time in about 20 years, though politicians want inflation lower before midterms.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C12-2|MT208-C12-2]] — The Fed is stuck: tightening enough to fight supply-driven inflation would break the bond market at the current dollar level.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C16-1|MT208-C16-1]] — Host expects recurring deflationary fits (like March 2020) met by large QE, UBI and eventually yield curve control.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C16-2|MT208-C16-2]] — The Fed will avoid yield curve control because it cannot be exited, preferring yield curve management.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C16-3|MT208-C16-3]] — The Fed already intervenes within a band: the 2019 repo spike was fixed in 24-48 hours by expanding the balance sheet, so the balance sheet is the release valve.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C17-X1|MT208-C17-X1]] — Gromen thinks the Fed will eventually be forced into yield curve control because yield curve management gets tighter, but it will delay it as long as possible; the timing depends on inflation and growth.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C18-2|MT208-C18-2]] — Gromen reframes Fed hikes as slowing the dollar's fall, and the lower the dollar goes the more room the Fed has to hike.
- [[MT208 - What if the Saudis Break the Petrodollar System- - Luke Gromen's Prescient Predictions fro#^MT208-C19-3|MT208-C19-3]] — Central bankers coordinate at the BIS (per a 2012 WSJ article) on keeping the dollar from rising, so policy is coordinated.

## [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390]]
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C10-4|MT210-C10-4]] — Incoming Fed chair Kevin Warsh (nominated, in May) views AI as a disinflationary productivity boom, supporting rate cuts.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C11-1|MT210-C11-1]] — Greenspan in 1996 held off hiking because of productivity gains, the precedent Warsh uses for cutting rates.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C12-2|MT210-C12-2]] — If AI is disinflationary, an inflation-targeting Fed would stimulate during an equity bubble, risking policy error and asset bubbles, with political pressure for cuts.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C12-4|MT210-C12-4]] — Alan sees reasonably strong risk of a Fed policy error later this year and into next, not this month.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C13-1|MT210-C13-1]] — Political threats to Powell mean a new Fed chair will have easing pressure in mind, though the FOMC is a committee.

## [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378]]
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C01-X1|MT211-C01-X1]] — The December 2025 FOMC cut 25 bp to 3.50-3.75% (third cut of the year) with dissents from both the hawkish and dovish sides, so the committee split on direction even though the cut itself was expected.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C02-1|MT211-C02-1]] — Around FOMC announcements uncertainty is high and prices can reverse sharply, so short-term models may do better avoiding the Fed window.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C02-2|MT211-C02-2]] — Wide dispersion in FOMC dot projections (from hikes to six cuts) signals a high-uncertainty Fed in 2026 and so more risk in rates trading.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C02-3|MT211-C02-3]] — Despite a third cut, Fed projections show core PCE at 2.6% and growth at 2.3% in 2026, i.e. still-high inflation with stronger growth than before.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C02-X1|MT211-C02-X1]] — A divided FOMC shows independence but sends a messy set of signals, so market participants cannot easily read the policy direction, which raises uncertainty around policy events.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C02-X2|MT211-C02-X2]] — Data gaps and technical distortions in the post-shutdown releases (acknowledged by Powell) make the incoming economic data an additional source of uncertainty for the January meeting.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C03-5|MT211-C03-5]] — Powell's remark that payroll growth may be overstated by about 60,000 a month implies the real job market is flat or negative, so labor data carries observational uncertainty.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C04-2|MT211-C04-2]] — A rule of thumb values 10-year yields as expected inflation plus real GDP growth (about 2.6 + 2.3 = 4.9) plus any term premium, putting fair value near the high 4s versus the market near 4.1-4.2%.

## [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva]]
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C01-2|MT212-C01-2]] — A currency board plus fiscal discipline ended Bulgaria's late-1990s hyperinflation (over 8,000%) and left debt at 24% of GDP, a historical example of stabilizing programs.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C01-3|MT212-C01-3]] — Argentina has had many IMF programs without lasting recovery; the current program under a reform-minded president is hoped to be the last.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C01-X1|MT212-C01-X1]] — The IMF's role is to lend quickly (capacity about $1 trillion) to finance balance-of-payments needs of countries in crisis, not to fund investment projects, so it acts as a cushion that stops a country 'about to crash'.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C02-X3|MT212-C02-X3]] — The US holds over 17% of IMF quotas and so is the only member with a veto, because major decisions require at least 85% of the vote; global crisis support therefore depends on US consent.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C03-3|MT212-C03-3]] — Announced US tariffs fell from about 23% in April 2025 to 17-18%, and actually collected tariffs are only 8-9%, so the trade shock is smaller than feared.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C03-4|MT212-C03-4]] — Tit-for-tat retaliation did not occur; if it had, the negative impact on global growth would have been much larger.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C04-1|MT212-C04-1]] — Global disinflation continues, but US tariff effects on prices have been muted so far, so the IMF is not yet declaring victory.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C04-X1|MT212-C04-X1]] — Inflation in Europe is back at target, while US disinflation is slower and exposed to tariff pass-through, so the IMF sees the inflation threat as more contained in Europe.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C05-2|MT212-C05-2]] — Geopolitical confrontation has increased the IMF members' appetite to cooperate, since cooperation can no longer be taken for granted.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C06-X1|MT212-C06-X1]] — Coordinated crisis response is harder to assemble now than in COVID, when emergency meetings produced a fast joint response, so the next shock may meet a slower and less coordinated policy reaction, especially with no fiscal buffers.
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C07-2|MT212-C07-2]] — Coordinated monetary and fiscal responses make a large difference compared with each country acting alone.

## [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35]]
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C04-4|MT213-C04-4]] — Rule of thumb: below about 4% inflation equities still do fine (commodities gain importance); above 4% there is material impact on fiscal policy and markets.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C11-3|MT213-C11-3]] — Rate forward curves rarely predict policy accurately, often pricing hikes or cuts that never occur, so forecasts must be revised and managers nimble.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C11-4|MT213-C11-4]] — In early 2026 the Citrini report pushed yields down in February, then the March strike in Iran reversed rates, moving the market from pricing cuts to possible hikes.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C14-3|MT213-C14-3]] — Policy responses and crashes are now faster (GFC slow, SVB overnight bank run, COVID record-short move), so shutoffs must be designed in up front.

## [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor]]
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C02-1|MT214-C02-1]] — Fed, ECB and BoE made only 13 quarter-point moves in the 11 years to 2021 versus 60 in the following 36 months, so fixed-income trading opportunity is about 20 times larger.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C04-1|MT214-C04-1]] — During the volatile, rapidly changing 2025 tariff policy environment, medium-term quant strategies struggle while high-frequency and short-term tactical strategies do better.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C05-2|MT214-C05-2]] — Fed-watching alpha disappears when monetary policy is on hold; edge depends on policy activity.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C06-3|MT214-C06-3]] — Policy chaos (tariff announce-and-postpone) should calm toward the midterms, making it easier for models to capture moves that ultimately prevail.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C07-1|MT214-C07-1]] — Tariffs are inflationary, so the Fed cannot be as dovish as it would like; a likely new hawkish chair (Warsh) adds to this.

## [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334]]
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C05-1|MT216-C05-1]] — The Treasury Secretary's focus on lowering bond yields signals a policy aim of lower yields, which coincided with a drop in US yields, though the move may instead reflect risk aversion.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C05-X1|MT216-C05-X1]] — The recent drop in US yields reflected investor risk aversion, but if tariffs and other Trump policies play out they could push inflation and yields up instead, so the yield direction is open.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C10-1|MT216-C10-1]] — Stock-bond correlation turns positive mainly in inflation shocks when both fall, which destroys the diversification that justified a 60/40.
- [[MT216 - Why Smart Investors Are Switching to Trend Following - Systematic Investor 334#^MT216-C13-2|MT216-C13-2]] — Broad uncertainty about valuation makes prices informative so investors learn continuously, creating trends; when certainty is high, as during the Fed put period up to 2020, trend followers did worse in rising equity markets.

## [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap]]
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C02-6|MT217-C02-6]] — A big producer price surprise (PPI +1.4% m/m, 6% y/y) signals inflation re-accelerating, a risk to a richly valued market.
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C02-7|MT217-C02-7]] — A 10-year yield around 4.5% has been a threshold above which markets in the past struggled.
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C04-X1|MT217-C04-X1]] — Travel companies cut guidance in the spring 2026 earnings season because of geopolitical uncertainty from the war (Expedia forecast weak Q2 bookings, kept full-year guidance, warned of downward revisions if macro stays unstable, stock -9%), so earnings guidance in travel is a real-time read on the war's demand effect.
- [[MT217 - Why This Market “Melt-Up” is Making Me Nervous - The Weekly Wrap#^MT217-C05-X2|MT217-C05-X2]] — The war has raised the oil price and put the energy and mining sectors in flux, so the sector is an active source of opportunities and risks.

## [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405]]
- [[MT218 - Why Trend Following Is Harder Than It Looks - Systematic Investor - Ep.405#^MT218-C02-1|MT218-C02-1]] — The new Fed chair Warsh was more hawkish than expected at his first FOMC, wants less forward guidance and a smaller balance sheet, and with high inflation there is little appetite for cuts.

## [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo]]
- [[MT219 - Why Trend Following Might Outlast the AI Arms Race - Mark Rzepczynski - Systematic Investo#^MT219-C05-2|MT219-C05-2]] — Woodford's natural rate of interest framework underlies central bank policy: raise or cut rates relative to the natural rate to affect aggregate demand and use forward guidance.

## [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365]]
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C03-1|MT220-C03-1]] — With SOFR near 3.4% and fed funds at 4.25-4.5%, rate cuts were priced in, so a trend in bonds needs a surprise relative to pricing (50bp cut or no cut), and the lack of trend reflects anticipation of the Fed.
- [[MT220 - Why Trend Following Never Looks the Same Twice - Systematic Investor - Ep.365#^MT220-C03-2|MT220-C03-2]] — Long yields can rise after rate cuts if investors consider cuts insufficient or fear inflation, since futures price future rates, not today's.

## [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381]]
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C16-2|MT221-C16-2]] — Prediction that US 10-year yields rise to 6% and then fall to 3.5% in 2026.
- [[MT221 - Why Trend Following Still Works in Tough Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.381#^MT221-C17-2|MT221-C17-2]] — Prediction that the Fed has to hike at least once in 2026 despite political pressure.

## [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391]]
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C02-X2|MT222-C02-X2]] — Governments with an electoral incentive manage energy prices lower (political agenda, intervention); this can hold prices down for a while, but when it stops working the market reasserts itself sharply, adding to the coiled-spring build-up in oil.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C04-1|MT222-C04-1]] — Oil-driven cost increases pass through transport, manufacturing, food and wages, and prices are sticky downward (fuel surcharges are not cut), so inflation lingers after the commodity retraces; central banks tend to hike quickly, as the 1970s tolerance was costly.
- [[MT222 - Why Trend Following Works - Systematic Investor - Ep.391#^MT222-C14-1|MT222-C14-1]] — A sustained oil spike would revive high inflation and interest rates (Australian cash 17.5% in the 1990s), producing significant trends and a healthy decade for trend following.

## [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373]]
- [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373#^MT223-C07-2|MT223-C07-2]] — If the 5x equity multiplier were right, a dollar of central bank buying is more effective in equities than bonds (25 cents), though with obvious moral hazard; QE's portfolio-rebalancing channel relies on this effect.
- [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373#^MT223-C11-1|MT223-C11-1]] — Rising inflation from a low level is good for equities, rising inflation from above-median level is bad; bonds always lose when inflation rises because yields rise.
- [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373#^MT223-C12-2|MT223-C12-2]] — Investors with inflation-plus mandates rarely use CTAs, yet active strategies such as CTAs with commodity exposure benefit from inflation volatility, unlike TIPS or real estate.
- [[MT223 - Why Trend Thrives When Inflation Returns - Systematic Investor - Ep.373#^MT223-C13-3|MT223-C13-3]] — TIPS and long-dated bonds combine inflation and duration exposure which offset (higher inflation brings higher yields); housing suffers from higher mortgage rates, so real assets do not reliably hedge inflation in the episode.

## [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411]]
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C02-2|MT224-C02-2]] — July 2026: multi-decade highs in long-dated yields in Japan, US and UK, Fed holding with three dissents and bond-market disappointment.
- [[MT224 - Why the Best Portfolios Are Built to Be Wrong - Systematic Investor - Ep. 411#^MT224-C14-X1|MT224-C14-X1]] — Central banks, governments and policy makers deliberately suppress volatility so that price-insensitive, rule-bound holders (banks, pensions, insurers) lever up in the assets policy wants to grow (government bonds, housing); suppressed volatility plus leverage is what produces the fragility and fat left tails.

## [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389]]
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C07-1|MT225-C07-1]] — The market has turned deflationary three times in six years, each time before a more inflationary push, because populism (demographics, K-shaped economy) drives a structurally higher interest-rate regime; AI is a catalyst for a political response, as Covid's brief disinflation preceded inflation.
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C07-X1|MT225-C07-X1]] — The AI-deflation scenario in the Citrini report ignores policy reaction (what the Fed would do and fiscal responses); the host expects the Fed to cut on disinflation (Warsh's argument that AI makes everything cheaper) and sees fiscal policy responding, so deflation scares tend to be answered by easier policy.
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C08-2|MT225-C08-2]] — Economic view: productivity gains from a technology raise the neutral rate via capex, so cutting rates on disinflation would be a mistake (Greenspan new paradigm of the 1990s).
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C14-X1|MT225-C14-X1]] — Policy by fait accompli: actions later found illegal (the tariffs) still shaped the economy and markets for a year and a half because the courts respond slowly, so the speaker treats a flood of fast, hard measures (including election controls before the midterms) as having real market effects regardless of legality.
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C15-X1|MT225-C15-X1]] — Markets have not reacted adversely to institutional and election-control moves (honestly never really have), but the guest argues the effect comes eventually through the weighing machine and may take years, and only after reflexive effects have played out.
- [[MT225 - Why the Market Isn’t Moving, And Why That’s Dangerous - Systematic Investor - Ep.389#^MT225-C16-X1|MT225-C16-X1]] — The administration offsets structural liquidity draws (refinancing of debt after the 2021 rate bottom, limits to short-end issuance and soft QE) with measures such as changes to the supplementary leverage ratio and the stablecoin act, but these only work if markets keep rising, otherwise the system gives out under its own weight.

## [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38]]
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C02-3|MT226-C02-3]] — Pinning rates at the floor and QE in the 2010s suppressed both economic and market volatility.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C02-X1|MT226-C02-X1]] — In the demand-starved 2010s, zero rates and QE were deliberately used to push investors out the risk curve because risk-free and low-risk assets were left with increasingly low returns, which supported risk assets and long-duration exposures.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C03-2|MT226-C03-2]] — Policy-suppressed volatility compressed cross-sectional market dispersion, shrinking the alpha opportunity set for active and hedge fund managers.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C03-4|MT226-C03-4]] — Policymakers react to the previous crisis: after the GFC fiscal response was seen as too small, so the 2020s response was large fiscal activism.
- [[MT226 - Why the Old Rules of Diversification Are Changing - Allocator - Ep.38#^MT226-C04-2|MT226-C04-2]] — Biden-era policymakers saw the risk as doing too little (scarring) rather than too much, but then found there are risks on the other side (inflation).

## [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330]]
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C05-X2|MT227-C05-X2]] — With the 10-year yield at 5%, a strong payrolls number is bad for equities because investors de-risk ahead of it and few will buy in front of a rates shock, so a strong jobs print can trigger the next leg down.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C07-1|MT227-C07-1]] — Speculation that the Fed times interventions to after March opex, aware of options expiration risk cycles.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C10-X1|MT227-C10-X1]] — Central banks may still control the long end of the curve, but if long yields decouple from central-bank control (a small sign in the UK) yields could move sharply against holders; the host raises this as the risk to the consensus that policy can contain the long end.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C12-1|MT227-C12-1]] — The Fed dominated outcomes when deflation was structural, but with inflation and growth objectives now in conflict it is in a box.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C12-2|MT227-C12-2]] — Guest expects Trump to name a shadow Fed chair early to pressure Powell, citing a Nixon analogy, so Fed independence is a risk.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C12-3|MT227-C12-3]] — The January jobs and CPI releases are unusually important given Fed uncertainty and narrative control.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C14-2|MT227-C14-2]] — Trump may be less hard on China than expected because of Russia-China alignment and statements on Taiwan cost, and tariffs may target Canada and Mexico instead.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C14-X1|MT227-C14-X1]] — Caveat to the guest's structural-inflation, rising-yield thesis: the only meaningful counter-argument he has seen is a US-China deal and China reversion, which could ease inflation pressure and change the macro picture.
- [[MT227 - Yield Storm Ahead- Preparing for a New Era - Systematic Investor 330#^MT227-C15-1|MT227-C15-1]] — Trump could do a deal with China (Nixon analog), which would be short-term supportive and reduce inflation pressure.
